<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
A \textbf{filtration} is a sequence of $\sigma$-Algebras $( \mathcal{F}_t )_{t\geq 0}$ such that for any $s < t$, we have $\mathcal{F}_s \subseteq \mathcal{F}_t \subseteq \mathcal{F}$.
\newline
We call a stochastic process $( X_t )_{t\geq 0}$ on $( \Omega, \mathcal{F}, \mathbb{P} )$ \textbf{adapted} to $( \mathcal{F}_t )_{t\geq 0}$ if, for any $t \geq 0$, $X_t$ is $\mathcal{F}_t$-measurable.
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- definition-2: body_md -->
A \textbf{stopping time} of the filtration $( \mathcal{F}_t )_{t\geq 0}$ is a random variable $T \colon \Omega \longrightarrow [0, \infty]$ such that at any given time $t\geq0$, we have $\{ T \leq t \} \in \mathcal{F}_t$.
<!-- end definition-2 -->
<!-- definition-3: body_md -->
We call a stochastic process $(X_t)_{t \geq 0}$ \textbf{uniformly integrable} if 
    \begin{align*}
        \underset{t \in [0, \infty)}{\sup} \, \mathbb{E} \Big[ |X_t| \mathbbm{1}_{\{|X_t| \geq \varepsilon\}} \Big] \xrightarrow[]{\varepsilon \rightarrow \infty}0.
    \end{align*}
<!-- end definition-3 -->
<!-- definition-4: body_md -->
A real-valued stochastic process $(X_t)_{t \geq 0}$ is called a \textbf{martingale} with respect to the filtration $(\mathcal{F}_t)_{t \geq 0}$ if  $(X_t)_{t \geq 0}$ is adapted to $(\mathcal{F}_t)_{t \geq 0}$ and
    \begin{itemize}
        \item [(i)] $\mathbb{E}\big[ | X_t| \big] < \infty \quad $for every $t \geq 0$ and
        \item [(ii)]  $\mathbb{E} \big[ X_t  | \mathcal{F}_s \big] = X_s \quad \mathbb{P} \text{-a.s.}$ for every $0 \leq s \leq t$.
    \end{itemize}
<!-- end definition-4 -->
<!-- definition-5: body_md -->
A stochastic process $M \equiv (M_t)_{t \geq 0}$ with $M_0 = 0$ a.s. and continuous sample paths that is adapted to the filtration $( \mathcal{F}_t )_{t\geq 0}$ is a \textbf{continuous local martingale} if there exists a nondecreasing sequence of stopping times $(T_n)_{n \geq 0}$ such that $T_n(\omega) \nearrow \infty$ for every $\omega \in \Omega$ and for every $n\geq 0$, the stopped process $M_{t \wedge T_n}$ is a uniformly integrable martingale.
<!-- end definition-5 -->
<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
Let $M \equiv (M_t)_{t \geq 0}$ be a continuous local martingale. Then there exists an increasing process $\left\langle  M\right \rangle \equiv (\left\langle  M\right \rangle_t)_{t \geq 0}$ that is unique up to sets with measure zero such that $(M^2_t - \left\langle  M\right \rangle_t)_{t \geq 0}$ is a continuous local martingale. Furthermore, for fixed $t>0$, let $E_n = \big \{ 0 = t^{(n)}_0 < t^{(n)}_1 <...< t^{(n)}_{m_n} = t \big \}$ be an arbitrary increasing subdivision of $[0, t]$ with
    \begin{align*}
        \underset{t_i \in E_n}{\sup} |t_i - t_{i+1}| \xrightarrow[]{n\rightarrow \infty}0.
    \end{align*}
    Then, we have 
    \begin{align*}
        \left\langle  M\right \rangle_t = \lim_{n \rightarrow \infty} \sum^{m_n-1}_{i = 0} \Big( M_{t^{(n)}_{i+1}} -M_{t^{(n)}_{i}} \Big)^2.
    \end{align*}
    We call $\left\langle  M\right \rangle$ the \textbf{quadratic variation} of $M$.
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
\cite[p. 80-84]{LeGall2016BrownianCalculus}.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- example-1: body_md -->
The Brownian motion $(B_t)_{t \geq 0}$ is a continuous local martingale and its quadratic variation is $\left \langle B \right \rangle_t = t$ for every $t \geq 0$.
    \label{BrownianExample}
<!-- end example-1 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
One can see immediately that Brownian motion is a martingale and its sample paths are by definition continuous. Therefore, Brownian motion is a continuous local martingale. For the computation of the quadratic variation, we refer the reader to \cite[Proposition 2.16]{LeGall2016BrownianCalculus}.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
For every $p>0$, there exist two constants $c_{\mathrm{BDG}}, C_{\mathrm{BDG}} >0$ depending only on $p$ such that, for every continuous local martingale $M$ that vanishes at $t = 0$ and every stopping time $T$
\begin{align*}
    c_{\mathrm{BDG}} \mathbb{E}\bigg[  \left\langle  M\right \rangle_T^{p/2} \bigg] \leq \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \left\|{M_t}\right\|^p \bigg] \leq C_{\mathrm{BDG}} \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \left\langle  M\right \rangle_T^{p/2} \bigg].
\end{align*}
\label{Burkholder}
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
\cite[p. 124-126]{LeGall2016BrownianCalculus}
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- definition-6: body_md -->
For $p \in [1,\infty)$, the \textbf{Wasserstein-$p$ distance} between two probability measures $\mu,\nu \in \mathcal P_p(\mathbb R^d)$ is defined as
    \begin{align*}
        W_p(\mu,\nu)
        :=
        \bigg( 
        \underset{\pi \in \Pi(\mu,\nu)}{\inf}
        \int_{\mathbb R^d \times \mathbb R^d} d(x,y)^p \, d\pi(x,y)
         \bigg)^{1/p},
    \end{align*}
    where
    \[
    \Pi(\mu,\nu)
    :=
    \big\{
    \pi \in \mathcal P(\mathbb R^d \times \mathbb R^d)
    :
    M_X\pi=\mu \text{ and } M_Y\pi=\nu
    \big\}.
    \]
    Here, $d(x,y)$ denotes a prescribed metric on $\mathbb R^d$; a common choice is $d(x,y)=\left\|{x-y}\right\|$. The measures
    \begin{align*}
        (M_X\pi)(A) := \int_{A \times \mathbb R^d} d\pi(x,y)
        \quad \text{and} \quad
        (M_Y\pi)(B) := \int_{\mathbb R^d \times B} d\pi(x,y),
    \end{align*}
    for all $A,B \in \mathcal B(\mathbb R^d)$, are called the \textbf{first} and \textbf{second marginal} of $\pi$, respectively.
    \label{Pre:Wasserstein}
<!-- end definition-6 -->
<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
For any $p \in [1,\infty)$, the Wasserstein-$p$ distance is a metric on $\mathcal P_p(\mathbb R^d)$. Moreover, the Wasserstein space $\big( \mathcal P_p(\mathbb R^d),W_p \big)$ is a complete separable metric space. \cite[Theorem 6.9, Theorem 6.18]{Villani2009OptimalTransport}
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
Let $(Z_i)_{i \in \mathbb{N}}$ be a collection of independent identically distributed real random variables such that $\mathbb{E} \big[ |Z_1|^r \big] < \infty$ for some $r \geq 2$. Then, for every $M > \mathbb{E}\big[ |Z_1| \big] $ there exists a constant $C > 0$, independent of $N$ such that for every $N \in \mathbb{N}$
    \begin{align*}
        \mathbb{P} \bigg( \frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i=1} Z_i \geq M \bigg) \leq CN^{-r/2}.
    \end{align*}
    \label{GerberLemma2.5}
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
Let $N \in \mathbb{N}, d \in \mathbb{N}$ and $Z_1, .., Z_N$ be independent, identically distributed Gaussian random vectors such that $Z_1 \sim \mathcal{N}(0, \sigma^2I_d)$
    Then, for every $p \geq 2$, there exists a constant $C_p >0$ depending only on $p$ such that
    \begin{align*}
          \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{1 \leq i \leq N}{\max} \left\|{Z_i}\right\|^p \bigg] \leq \sigma^pC_p \Big( d\log (2Nd) \Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}.
    \end{align*}
\label{Pre:LemmaNormalVectors}
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- definition-7: body_md -->
Let $\big( \Omega, \mathcal{F}, \mathbb{P} \big)$ be a probability space. An \textbf{interacting particle system} of size $N\in \mathbb{N}$ \textbf{in discrete time} is a collection of $N$ stochastic processes 
    \begin{align*}
   \big(\mathcal{X}^N_k \big)_k = \big(X^i_k\big)^N_k = \big ( X^1_k, ..., X^N_k  \big)_{k \in \mathbb{N}} ,
\end{align*}
where for each $i \leq N$ at each time point $k$, $X^i_k \colon \Omega \longrightarrow \mathbb{R}^d$ is a random variable in $\big( \Omega, \mathcal{F}, \mathbb{P} \big)$.
<!-- end definition-7 -->
<!-- definition-8: body_md -->
Let $\big( \Omega, \mathcal{F}, \mathbb{P} \big)$ be a probability space. An \textbf{interacting particle system} of size $N\in \mathbb{N}$ \textbf{in continuous time} is a collection of $N$ stochastic processes 
    \begin{align*}
   \big(\mathcal{X}^N_t \big)_t =   \big(X^i_t\big)^N_t = \big ( X^1_t, ..., X^N_t  \big)_{t \geq 0},
\end{align*}
where for each $i \leq N$ at each time $t$, $X^i_t \colon \Omega \longrightarrow \mathbb{R}^d$ is a random variable in $\big( \Omega, \mathcal{F}, \mathbb{P} \big)$.
\label{Pre:IPScts}
<!-- end definition-8 -->
<!-- definition-9: body_md -->
For a collection of random variables $\mathcal{X}^N = (X^1, ..., X^N)$, we define the \textbf{empirical measure} as
    \begin{align*}
        \mu_{\mathcal{X}^N} \colon \Omega^N &\longrightarrow \mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^d) \\
       (\omega_1, ..., \omega_N) &\longmapsto \frac{1}{N} \sum_{i=1}^{N} \delta_{X^i (\omega_i)}.
    \end{align*}
    \label{Pre:EmpMeas}
<!-- end definition-9 -->
<!-- proposition-1: body_md -->
The empirical measure
    \[
    \mu_{\mathcal X^N}\colon (\Omega^N,\mathcal F^N)\to \bigl(\mathcal P(\mathbb R^d),\mathcal C(\mathcal P(\mathbb R^d))\bigr)
    \]
    is a random variable, where $\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^d))$ denotes the $\sigma$-Algebra over $\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^d)$ generated by all sets of the form $\big \{ \mu \in \mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^d) : \mu(B) \leq y \big \}$, for $B \in \mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^d)$ and $y \in [0, 1]$.
    \label{Pre:PropEmpMeas}
<!-- end proposition-1 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
For $B \in \mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^d)$ we define the function 
    \begin{align*}
      f_B\colon \mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^d) \longrightarrow [0, 1],  \quad f_B(\mu) := \mu(B). 
    \end{align*}
    Then $\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^d))$ is generated by
    \begin{align*}
        \big \{ f_B^{-1}(A) : A \in \mathcal{B}([0, 1]), \, B \in \mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^d)\big \}.
    \end{align*}
    Therefore, we fix $B \in \mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^d)$ and consider arbitrary $A \in \mathcal{B}([0, 1])$. We can immediately deduce the equality
    \begin{align*}
        \big \{ \omega \in \Omega^N  : \mu_{\mathcal{X}^N}(\omega) \in f^{-1}_B(A)\big \} = \big \{ \omega \in \Omega^N  : \big( \mu_{\mathcal{X}^N}(\omega) \big) (B) \in A \big \}, 
    \end{align*}
    where we used the fact that $f_B^{-1}(A)$ denotes the set of all probability measures $\mu \in \mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^d)$ such that $\mu (B) \in A$. We can use the definition of the empirical measure to further compute 
    \begin{align*}
        \big \{ \omega \in \Omega^N  : \big( \mu_{\mathcal{X}^N}(\omega) \big) (B) \in A \big \} &= \bigg \{ \omega \in \Omega^N  : \bigg( \frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i=1} \delta_{X^i(\omega_i)} \bigg) (B) \in A \bigg \} \\
        &= \bigg \{ \omega \in \Omega^N  : \bigg( \frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i=1} \mathbbm{1}_B(X^i(\omega_i)) \bigg) \in A \bigg \}. 
    \end{align*}
    This set belongs to $\mathcal{F}^N$, which follows from the measurability of each random variable $X^i$ and the measurability of the indicator functions and, therefore, their sum. By the fact that we have chosen $A$ and $B$ to be arbitrary and that  $\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^d))$ is generated by \[\big \{ f^{-1}_B(A) : A \in \mathcal{B}([0, 1]), B \in \mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^d)\big \},\] we have shown that 
    \begin{align*}
        \big \{ \omega \in \Omega^N : \mu_{\mathcal{X}^N}(\omega) \in C \big \} \in \mathcal{F}^N, \quad \forall C \in \mathcal{C}(\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^d)), 
    \end{align*}
    and therefore $\mu_{\mathcal{X}^N}$ is a random variable from $\big( \Omega^N, \mathcal{F}^N \big)$ to $\big( \mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^d), \mathcal{C}(\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^d)) \big)$.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- example-2: body_md -->
\label{WasserBD}
    For two collections of random variables $\mathcal{X}^N, \mathcal{Y}^N$ of size $N \in \mathbb{N}$, the $p$th power of the Wasserstein-$p$ distance between their empirical measures can be estimated as follows.
    \begin{align*}
   \mathbb{E}\,  \Big[ W^p_p(\mu_{\mathcal{X}^N}, \mu_{\mathcal{Y}^N}) \Big] = \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{\pi \in S_N}{\min} \frac{1}{N} \sum_{i=1}^{N} \left\|{X^i - Y^{\pi (i)}}\right\|^p \bigg] &\leq  \frac{1}{N} \sum_{i=1}^{N} \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^i - Y^i}\right\|^p \bigg] \notag \\&\leq \underset{1 \leq i \leq N}{\max} \,  \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^i- Y^i}\right\|^p \bigg]. 
\end{align*}
<!-- end example-2 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
Let $1 \leq p<q$ and $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho \in \mathcal{P}_q(\mathbb{R}^d)$ with $\mathbb{E}_{\overline{\rho}} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^q \big]\leq M_q$. Define the particle system $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\mathcal{X}^N$ by random variables $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^1, ..., \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^N$ that are drawn independently from $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho$. Then there exists a constant $C = C(p, q, d)$ such that for every $N \geq 1$
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \big[ W^p_p(\mu_{\overline{\mathcal{X}}^N}, \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho) \big] \leq C M_q^{p/q} \begin{cases}
             N^{-\frac{1}{2}} + N^{- \frac{q-p}{q}} \quad &\text{if } p> \frac{d}{2} \text{ and } p \neq \frac{q}{2}, \\
             N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\log (1+ N) + N^{- \frac{q-p}{q}} \quad &\text{if } p = \frac{d}{2} \text{ and } p \neq \frac{q}{2}, \\
             N^{-\frac{p}{d}} + N^{- \frac{q-p}{q}} \quad &\text{if } p \in (0, \frac{d}{2}) \text{ and } q \neq \frac{d}{d-p}.
         \end{cases}
\end{align*}
\label{Pre:ConvEmp}
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- definition-10: body_md -->
Let $p \in [1, \infty)$ and let $\big( \rho^N \big)_N$ be a collection of measures on $\big( \mathbb{R}^d \big)^N$. Then we say that \textbf{propagation of chaos in Wasserstein-$p$ distance} towards the probability measure $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho \in \mathcal{P}\big( \mathbb{R}^d \big)$ holds, if
\begin{align*}
    W_p\big( \rho^N, \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^{\otimes N} \big) \xrightarrow[]{N \rightarrow \infty}0.
\end{align*}
We call $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho$ the \textbf{mean-field distribution} or \textbf{mean-field limit} of $\big( \rho^N \big)_N$.\newline
For flows of probability measures $\rho_{[0, T]}^N := (\rho^N_t)_{t \in [0, T]} \in \mathcal{P}\big( C([0, T];\mathbb{R}^d)^N \big)$, we say that propagation of chaos uniformly in time over $[0, T]$ towards $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_{[0, T]} \in \mathcal{P}\big( C([0, T];\mathbb{R}^d) \big)$ holds, if
\begin{align*}
    W_p\Big( \rho_{[0, T]}^N, \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_{[0, T]}^{\otimes N} \Big) \xrightarrow[]{N \rightarrow \infty}0,
\end{align*}
and propagation of chaos pointwise in time over $[0, T]$ holds if
\begin{align*}
  \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \, W_p\big( \rho_t^N, \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_t^{\otimes N} \big) \xrightarrow[]{N \rightarrow \infty}0.
\end{align*}
<!-- end definition-10 -->
<!-- definition-11: body_md -->
Fix $T \in (0, \infty]$. For every $N \in \mathbb{N}$, let $\mathcal{X}^N_T$ be a $N$-particle system with $\operatorname*{Law}\big( X^1_t, \dots, X^N_t\big) \in \mathcal{P}\big((\mathbb{R}^d)^N\big)$ for every $0 \leq t \leq T$. We say that \textbf{propagation of chaos by coupling} holds for this system if:\\
For every $N \in \mathbb{N}$, there exists an independent particle system $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\mathcal{X}^N_T$, i.e. a collection of $N$ independent stochastic processes $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\mathcal{X}^N_T = (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^1_t, ..., \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^N_t)_{t \in [0, T]}$, and some $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_t \in \mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^d)$ such that $\operatorname*{Law}\big( \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^1_t, \dots \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^N_t\big) = \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_t^{\otimes N}$ for every $0 \leq t \leq T$ and a $\varepsilon(N, T)>0$ with $\varepsilon(N, T) \xrightarrow[]{N \rightarrow \infty}0$ such that
\begin{align*}
    &\frac{1}{N} \sum_{i=1}^{N} \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_t - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p  \bigg] \leq \varepsilon(N, T) \quad (\text{pathwise}) \\
    \text{or} \quad & \frac{1}{N} \sum_{i=1}^{N}  \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \,\mathbb{E}  \bigg[  \left\|{X^i_t - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \bigg] \leq \varepsilon(N, T) \quad (\text{pointwise}).
\end{align*}
In this case, we will refer to $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_{[0, T]}$ as the \textbf{mean-field law} of $\mathcal{X}^N_T$, where $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_{[0,T]}$ denotes the pathwise law of one nonlinear process with time marginals $(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_t)_{t\in[0,T]}$.
\label{Pre:ChaosCoup}
<!-- end definition-11 -->
<!-- remark-2: body_md -->
In the last step, we bound the infimum by the specific coupling given by the joint law of $(\mathcal{X}^N_T, \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\mathcal{X}^N_T)$ from Definition \ref{Pre:ChaosCoup}, which is an admissible coupling since its marginals are $\rho^N_{[0,T]}$ and $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^{\otimes N}_{[0,T]}$ by construction. More precisely, under this coupling,
\begin{align*}
    \int_{\mathcal{C}([0,T];(\mathbb{R}^d)^N)\times \mathcal{C}([0,T];(\mathbb{R}^d)^N)}
    \frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i=1} \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \left\|{x^i_t - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{x}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{x}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}x^i_t}\right\|^p \, d \pi(x,\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{x}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{x}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}x)
    = \frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i =1} \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_t - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \bigg].
\end{align*}
<!-- end remark-2 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
Let $\phi: [0, T] \rightarrow [0,\infty)$ be bounded and measurable and let there be constants $\alpha \geq 0, \beta >0$ such that
    \begin{align*}
        \phi(t) \leq \alpha + \beta \int^t_0 \phi(\tau) \, d \tau \quad \forall t \in [0, T].
    \end{align*}
Then, for all $t \in [0, T]$
\begin{align*}
    \phi(t) \leq \alpha e^{\beta t}.
\end{align*}
\label{GronwallLemma}
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
\cite[p. 310]{Walter1998OrdinaryEquations}
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- definition-12: body_md -->
Let $f \colon \mathbb{R}^d \longrightarrow \mathbb{R}$ be a proper function. The \textbf{consensus point} of a probability measure $\rho \in \mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^d)$ is defined as
    \begin{align*}
        X^\alpha \colon \mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^d) &\longrightarrow \mathbb{R}^d, \quad
        \rho \longmapsto \frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}^d} x\omega_\alpha (x) d\rho(x)}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\rho})}},
    \end{align*}
    where $\omega_\alpha(x) := \exp(-\alpha f(x))$.
<!-- end definition-12 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
Let $f \in C_b(\mathbb{R}^d; \mathbb{R})$ nonnegative and attain its unique global minimum at $x^* \in \mathbb{R}^d$. Then, if $\rho \in \mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^d)$ is absolutely continuous with respect to the Lebesgue measure with $x^* \in \operatorname{supp}(\rho)$, we have
\begin{align*}
    \underset{\alpha \rightarrow \infty}{\lim} \Bigg(  - \frac{1}{\alpha} \log \bigg( \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} e^{-\alpha f} d \rho \bigg) \Bigg) = f(x^*).
\end{align*}
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- remark-3: body_md -->
Note that these estimates are not uniform over infinite time horizons, as the above $\varepsilon(N, T)$ grows exponentially with $T$.
<!-- end remark-3 -->
<!-- remark-4: body_md -->
Throughout this thesis, whenever we assert propagation of chaos in the Wasserstein-$p$ distance, we shall, unless explicitly stated otherwise, assume that the objective function $f$ satisfies conditions \eqref{A1}, \eqref{A2}, and \eqref{A3}. We emphasize that the said assumptions do not depend on the order $p$ of the Wasserstein distance or on the moments we work with.
<!-- end remark-4 -->
<!-- remark-5: body_md -->
The setting \eqref{A1}--\eqref{A3} is the special case $s = 1$, $\ell = u = 2$ of the assumptions in \cite[Section 2.1]{Gerber2025Mean-fieldSampling}, where the local Lipschitz condition is allowed to grow like $(1 + \left\|{x}\right\| + \left\|{y}\right\|)^s$ and the objective function is allowed to grow like $\left\|{x}\right\|^\ell$ from below and like $\left\|{x}\right\|^u$ from above. For the sake of simplicity, we restrict ourselves to the case $s = 1$, $\ell = u = 2$ and refer to \cite[Section 2.1]{Gerber2025Mean-fieldSampling} for the general case.
<!-- end remark-5 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{CarrL1}
 Let f fulfill (\ref{A1}), (\ref{A2}) and $\rho \in \mathcal{P}_2(\mathbb{R}^d)$ with $\mathbb{E}_\rho \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^2 \big] \leq M_2$. \\
  Then
  \begin{align*}
      \frac{\omega_\alpha(x^*)}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\rho})}} \leq e^{\alpha C_2(1+M_2)}.
  \end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
The proof follows as a direct consequence of Jensen's inequality combined with assumption \eqref{A2} imposed on the objective function $f$. We begin by invoking Jensen's inequality to obtain the estimate
    \begin{align*}
        \frac{\omega_\alpha(x^*)}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\rho})}} = \frac{e^{-\alpha f(x^*)}}{\int_{\mathbb{R}^d} e^{-\alpha f(x)} d\rho(x)} &= \bigg( \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} e^{-\alpha (f(x)- f(x^*))} d\rho(x) \bigg)^{-1}\\ \text{(Jensen)} \quad &\leq \bigg( e^{- \alpha \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} f(x) - f(x^*) d\rho(x)} \bigg)^{-1}.
    \end{align*}
We now rearrange and use \eqref{A2}:
\begin{align*}
    \bigg( e^{- \alpha \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} f(x) - f(x^*) d\rho(x)} \bigg)^{-1} = e^{ \alpha \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} f(x) - f(x^*) d\rho(x)} &\leq e^{ \alpha \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} C_2(1 + \left\|{x}\right\|^2) d\rho(x)}\\ &= e^{ \alpha C_2 \big( 1+ \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \left\|{x}\right\|^2 d\rho(x) \big)}.
\end{align*}
Finally, we conclude by using the moment bound of $\rho$:
\begin{align*}
    e^{ \alpha C_2 \big( 1+ \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \left\|{x}\right\|^2 d\rho(x) \big)} \leq e^{\alpha C_2 (1+M_2)}.
\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
Let $p \geq 1$, let $f$ fulfill \eqref{A1}, \eqref{A2} and \eqref{A3}, and let $\mu, \nu \in \mathcal{P}_p(\mathbb{R}^d)$ such that there exists a $M > 0$ with $\mathbb{E}_\mu \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big]\leq M$ and $\mathbb{E}_\nu \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big] \leq M$. Then there exists a constant $C_0 = C_0(\alpha, p, M, C_1, C_2, C_3, R)$ such that
    \begin{align*}
        \left\|{X^\alpha(\mu) - X^\alpha(\nu) }\right\| \leq C_0W_p(\mu, \nu).
    \end{align*}
    \label{CarrL2}
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
Let $p\geq 1$, let f fulfill (\ref{A1}), (\ref{A2}), (\ref{A3}) and let $\rho \in \mathcal{P}_p(\mathbb{R}^d)$ and define the consensus measure $\eta^\alpha := \frac{\omega_\alpha \rho}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\rho})}}$\footnote{$\eta^\alpha$ is a probability measure on $\mathbb{R}^d$, since $0 < \omega_\alpha \leq \omega_\alpha(x^*)$ and hence $0 < \left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\rho})} < \infty$.}. Then there exists a constant $b_p>0$ depending only on $(\alpha, p, C_2, C_3, R)$ such that
   \begin{align*}
       \mathbb{E}_{\eta^\alpha} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big] \leq b_p\,  \mathbb{E}_\rho \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big].
   \end{align*}
    \label{GerberPropA4}
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
Let $p \geq 1$, let $f$ fulfill (\ref{A1}), (\ref{A2}), (\ref{A3}), and let $\rho \in \mathcal{P}_p(\mathbb{R}^d)$ with $\eta^\alpha := \frac{\omega_\alpha \rho}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\rho})}}$. Then there exists a constant $b_p>0$ depending only on $(\alpha, p, C_2, C_3, R)$ such that
    \begin{align*}
       \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho)}\right\|^p \leq  b_p\,  \mathbb{E}_\rho \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big].
    \end{align*}
    \label{GerberPropA4Corollary}
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
First, we use the definition of the consensus point:
    \begin{align*}
        \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho)}\right\|^p
        = \left\|{\int x \frac{\omega_\alpha(x)}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\rho})}} d \rho(x)}\right\|^p
        = \left\|{\mathbb{E}_{\eta^\alpha} [x]}\right\|^p.
    \end{align*}
    Since $\left\|{\cdot}\right\|^p$ is convex, Jensen's inequality and Lemma \ref{GerberPropA4} with exponent $p$ yield
    \begin{align*}
        \left\|{\mathbb{E}_{\eta^\alpha} [x]}\right\|^p
        \leq \mathbb{E}_{\eta^\alpha} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big]
        \leq b_p\, \mathbb{E}_\rho \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big].
    \end{align*}
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
Let $\nu \in \mathcal{P}_p(\mathbb{R}^d)$ for $p \geq 2$ and let for every $N \in \mathbb{N}$, $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^1, ..., \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^N$ be independent, identically distributed random variables such that $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i \sim \nu$ and let $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\nu}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\nu}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\nu^N = \frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i=1} \delta_{\overline{X}^i}$ denote their empirical measure. Furthermore, let there exist a $M_p > 0$ such that $\mathbb{E}_\nu \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big] \leq M_p$. For some $M>0$, define the event
    \begin{align*}
        \Omega_M := \bigg\{ \omega \in \Omega : \frac{1}{N} \sum_{i=1}^N \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i}\right\|^p \leq M \bigg\}.
    \end{align*}
    Then, there exists a constant $C_{\mathrm{EM}} >0$ independent of $N$ such that
    \begin{align*}
        \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\nu}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\nu}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\nu^N) - X^\alpha(\nu)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg] \leq C_{\mathrm{EM}} N^{-\frac{p}{2}}.
    \end{align*}
\label{CH:ConsBound}
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
Let $T > 0$ be the final time. Consider the interacting particle systems $\mathcal{X}^N_{T}$ and $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\mathcal{X}^N_{T}$ defined by the SDE systems \eqref{Delta:Scheme} and \eqref{Delta:MFScheme}, respectively, with initial data following the distribution $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_0 \in \mathcal{P}_p(\mathbb{R}^d)$. Then there exist constants $C_{\mathrm{D}} > 0$ and $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{C}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}C_{\mathrm{D}} > 0$ independent of $N$ such that for every $N \in \mathbb{N}$ and every $i \leq N$
    \begin{align*}
         \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \left\|{X_t^i}\right\|^p \bigg] \leq C_{\mathrm{D}} \quad \text{and} \quad \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_t^i}\right\|^p \bigg] \leq \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{C}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}C_{\mathrm{D}}.
    \end{align*}
    \label{NPDelta:UniformMoment}
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
We present the proof only for the interacting particle system $\mathcal{X}^N_{T}$. The proof for $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\mathcal{X}^N_{T}$ proceeds analogously.
Fix $\Tilde{T} \in [0, T]$. We begin by computing for an arbitrary $i \leq N$ on a path $\omega \in \Omega$
\begin{align*}
    \underset{t \in [0, \Tilde{T}]}{\sup} \left\|{X_t^i}\right\| &= \underset{t \in [0, \Tilde{T}]}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_0 + \int^t_0 - s \lambda (X^i_\tau - X^\alpha(\rho^N_\tau))\, d\tau + \sqrt{s} \delta \int^t_0 d B^i_\tau}\right\|\\ 
    &\leq \left\|{X^i_0}\right\| + s\lambda \int^{\Tilde{T}}_0\left\|{X^i_\tau}\right\| \, d\tau + s\lambda \int^{\Tilde{T}}_0 \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_\tau) }\right\| \, d\tau + \sqrt{s}\delta \underset{t \in [0, \Tilde{T}]}{\sup} \left\|{B^i_t}\right\|.
\end{align*}
We use $(a+b+c+d)^p \leq 4^{p-1} (a^p+b^p+c^p+d^p)$ on the latter to estimate the $p$th moment
\begin{align*}
    &\underset{t \in [0, \Tilde{T}]}{\sup} \left\|{X_t^i}\right\|^p \\  \leq \quad &4^{p-1} \Bigg( \left\|{X^i_0}\right\|^p + s^p\lambda^p \bigg( \int^{\Tilde{T}}_0\left\|{X^i_\tau}\right\| \, d\tau \bigg)^p + s^p\lambda^p \bigg( \int^{\Tilde{T}}_0 \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_\tau) }\right\| \, d\tau \bigg)^p + s^{\frac{p}{2}}\delta^p \underset{t \in [0, \Tilde{T}]}{\sup} \left\|{B^i_t}\right\|^p \Bigg).
\end{align*}
Using Hölders inequality on the two integral terms and taking expectations on both sides leaves us with
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, \Tilde{T}]}{\sup} \left\|{X_t^i}\right\|^p \bigg] &\leq 4^{p-1} \Bigg(  \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^i_0}\right\|^p \bigg] + s^p\lambda^p {\Tilde{T}}^{p-1} \int^{\Tilde{T}}_0 \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{X^i_\tau}\right\|^p \Big] \, d\tau   \\ & \quad + s^p\lambda^p {\Tilde{T}}^{p-1} \int^{\Tilde{T}}_0 \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_\tau) }\right\|^p \Big] \, d\tau + s^{\frac{p}{2}}\delta^p \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, \Tilde{T}]}{\sup} \left\|{B^i_t}\right\|^p \Bigg] \Bigg).
\end{align*}
We use Lemma \ref{Burkholder} in order to bound the Brownian motion part with a constant $C_{\mathrm{BDG}}$ that depends only on $p$, i.e.
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, \Tilde{T}]}{\sup} \left\|{B^i_t}\right\|^p \Bigg] \leq C_{\mathrm{BDG}}(d{\Tilde{T}})^{\frac{p}{2}}.
    \end{align*}
Together with absorbing the constants into one constant $C = C(s, \lambda, \delta, {\Tilde{T}}, d, p)$ yields
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, \Tilde{T}]}{\sup} \left\|{X_t^i}\right\|^p \bigg] \leq C \Bigg(  \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^i_0}\right\|^p \bigg] + \int^{\Tilde{T}}_0 \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{X^i_\tau}\right\|^p \Big] \, d\tau + \int^{\Tilde{T}}_0 \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_\tau) }\right\|^p \Big] \, d\tau + 1 \Bigg).
    \label{NPDelta:B1}
\end{align}
We can see that we are almost there to use Grönwall's lemma to finish the proof. But first, we need to get a bound on the consensus point part. We use Corollary \ref{GerberPropA4Corollary} to estimate
\begin{align*}
    \int^{\Tilde{T}}_0 \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_\tau) }\right\|^p \Big] \, d\tau &\leq \int^{\Tilde{T}}_0 b_p \mathbb{E} \Big[  \mathbb{E}_{\rho^N_\tau} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big] \Big] \, d \tau.\\
\end{align*}
Combining the latter with (\ref{NPDelta:B1}) gives us for a constant $C = C(s, \lambda, \delta, {\Tilde{T}}, d, p, b_p)$\footnote{Note that we abuse the notation here in the way that $C$ displays a constant that just depends on model parameters.}
\begin{align*}
    &\mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, \Tilde{T}]}{\sup} \left\|{X_t^i}\right\|^p \bigg] \\ \leq \, & C \Bigg(  1+ \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^i_0}\right\|^p \bigg] + \int^{\Tilde{T}}_0 \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{X^i_\tau}\right\|^p \Big] \, d\tau + \int^{\Tilde{T}}_0 \mathbb{E} \Big[  \mathbb{E}_{\rho^N_\tau} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big] \Big] \, d\tau  \Bigg) \\
    \leq \, &C \Bigg(  1+ \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^i_0}\right\|^p \bigg] + \int^{\Tilde{T}}_0 \mathbb{E} \Bigg[  \underset{s\in [0, \tau]}{\sup}\left\|{X^i_s}\right\|^p \Bigg] \, d\tau + \int^{\Tilde{T}}_0 \mathbb{E} \Bigg[  \underset{s\in [0, \tau]}{\sup}\mathbb{E}_{\rho^N_s} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big] \Bigg] \, d\tau  \Bigg)\\
    \leq \, &C \Bigg(  1+ \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^i_0}\right\|^p \bigg] + 2\int^{\Tilde{T}}_0 \mathbb{E} \Bigg[  \underset{s\in [0, \tau]}{\sup}\left\|{X^i_s}\right\|^p \Bigg] \, d\tau  \Bigg),
\end{align*}
where we used in the last line that
\begin{align}
     \mathbb{E} \Bigg[  \underset{s\in [0, \tau]}{\sup}\mathbb{E}_{\rho^N_s} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big] \Bigg] =  \mathbb{E} \Bigg[  \underset{s\in [0, \tau]}{\sup}\frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{j=1} \left\|{X^j_s}\right\|^p \Bigg] \leq \mathbb{E} \Bigg[  \frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{j=1} \underset{s\in [0, \tau]}{\sup} \left\|{X^j_s}\right\|^p \Bigg] = \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \underset{s\in [0, \tau]}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_s}\right\|^p \Bigg]. \label{NPDeltaEmpB1}
\end{align}
Lastly, an application of Grönwall's lemma (Lemma \ref{GronwallLemma}) yields
\begin{align}
     \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \left\|{X_t^i}\right\|^p \bigg] \leq C\bigg( 1+ \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^i_0}\right\|^p \bigg] \bigg)e^{2CT} = \colon C_{\mathrm{D}},
\end{align}
with $C_{\mathrm{D}} = C(s, \lambda, \delta, T, d, p, b_p)$.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- remark-6: body_md -->
The auxiliary step (\ref{NPDeltaEmpB1}) immediately gives us a uniform-in-time bound for the expected $p$th moment of the empirical measure of $\mathcal{X}^N_{T}$:
    \begin{align}
        \mathbb{E} \Bigg[  \underset{t\in [0, T]}{\sup}\mathbb{E}_{\rho^N_t} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big] \Bigg] \leq \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \left\|{X_t^i}\right\|^p \bigg] \leq C_{\mathrm{D}}.
    \end{align}
<!-- end remark-6 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
Let $T >0$ be a fixed final time. Consider the interacting particle systems $\mathcal{X}^N_{T}$ and $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\mathcal{X}^N_{T}$ defined by the SDE systems \eqref{Delta:Scheme} and \eqref{Delta:MFScheme}, respectively, with initial data following the distribution $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_0 \in \mathcal{P}_p(\mathbb{R}^d)$. For a fixed \(M>0\), define the set \begin{align*}
       \Omega_M := \bigg\{ \omega \in \Omega : \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \,  \frac{1}{N} \sum_{i=1}^{N} \max \Big\{ \left\|{X^i_t}\right\|^p , \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \Big \} \leq M \bigg\}.
   \end{align*} Then there exists a constant $C>0$ independent of $N$ such that
    \begin{align*}
        \mathbb{P}(\Omega_M) \geq 1 - \frac{C}{M}.
    \end{align*}
    \label{MFD:Moment}
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
We obtain with Markov's inequality 
\begin{align*}
        \mathbb{P}(\Omega^c_M) &\leq \mathbb{P}\Bigg( \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Big( \left\|{X^i_t}\right\|^p + \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \Big) > M \Bigg) \\
        &= \mathbb{P}\Bigg( \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \sum_{i=1}^{N}\Big( \left\|{X^i_t}\right\|^p + \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \Big) > NM \Bigg)\\
        &\leq \frac{\mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|{X^i_t}\right\|^p\bigg]}{NM} + \frac{\mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|{ \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \bigg]}{NM}\\
        &\leq \frac{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_t}\right\|^p \bigg]}{NM} + \frac{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \left\|{ \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \bigg ]}{NM}\\
        &\leq \frac{\underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max}\, \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_t}\right\|^p \bigg ]}{M} + \frac{\underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max}\, \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \left\|{ \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p  \bigg]}{M}.
\end{align*}
We use Lemma \ref{NPDelta:UniformMoment} to obtain 
\begin{align*}
   \underset{1 \leq i \leq N}{\max}\, \mathbb{E} \Bigg[  \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_t}\right\|^p  \Bigg] \leq C_{\mathrm{D}}, \quad \quad 
   \underset{1 \leq i \leq N}{\max}\, \mathbb{E} \Bigg[  \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \Bigg] \leq \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{C}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}C_{\mathrm{D}}
\end{align*}
We set $\frac{C}{2} \coloneqq \max \Big \{ C_{\mathrm{D}}, \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{C}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}C_{\mathrm{D}} \Big \}$ and get
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\underset{1 \leq i \leq N}{\max}\, \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_t}\right\|^p \bigg ]}{M} + \frac{\underset{1 \leq i \leq N}{\max}\, \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \left\|{ \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p  \bigg]}{M} \leq \frac{C}{M}.
\end{align*}
Therefore, we have 
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{P}(\Omega_M) \geq 1 - \frac{C}{M}.
\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
Let $\mathcal{X}_T^N$ and $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\mathcal{X}_T^N$ be defined by the $\delta$-CBO systems (\ref{Delta:Scheme}) and (\ref{Delta:MFScheme}), respectively, both sharing the same initial data $\mathcal{X}^N_0 = \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\mathcal{X}^N_0$ almost surely, with common distribution $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_0 \in \mathcal{P}_p(\mathbb{R}^d)$ for some $p \geq 2$. For a fixed \(M>0\), define the set
    \begin{align*}
       \Omega_M := \bigg\{ \omega \in \Omega : \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \,  \frac{1}{N} \sum_{i=1}^{N} \max \Big\{ \left\|{X^i_t}\right\|^p , \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \Big \} \leq M \bigg\}.
   \end{align*} 
   Then there exists a $\varepsilon^{\mathrm{rest}}_{\mathrm{D}}(N, T)>0$ with $\varepsilon^{\mathrm{rest}}_{\mathrm{D}}(N, T) \xrightarrow{N \rightarrow \infty} 0$ such that for every $N \in \mathbb{N}$
    \begin{align*}
        \underset{1 \leq i \leq N}{\max} \,  \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \,\left\|{ X^i_t - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg] \leq \varepsilon^{\mathrm{rest}}_{\mathrm{D}}(N, T).
    \end{align*}
    Consequently, if $\mathbb{P}(\Omega_M)>0$, then
    \begin{align*}
        \underset{1 \leq i \leq N}{\max} \,  \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \,\left\|{ X^i_t - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \Big | \Omega_M  \bigg] \leq \frac{\varepsilon^{\mathrm{rest}}_{\mathrm{D}}(N, T)}{\mathbb{P}(\Omega_M)}.
    \end{align*}
    \label{Delta:Thrm}
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
We fix some $N \in \mathbb{N}$, $i \leq N$. Since both particle systems share the same Brownian motion, 
the diffusion terms cancel exactly in the difference 
\begin{align*}
dF^i_t := d(X^i_t - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t) = -s \lambda (X^i_t -\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t)dt + s\lambda (X^\alpha(\rho^N_t) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_t))dt, \quad \forall t \in [0, T]
\end{align*}
leaving the deterministic 
linear integral equation
\begin{align*}
    F^i_t = s\lambda \int_0^t e^{-s\lambda(t-\tau)} D_\tau\, d\tau, 
    \quad D_\tau := X^\alpha(\rho^N_\tau) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_\tau),
\end{align*}
where we used $F^i_0 = X^i_0 - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_0 = 0$ almost 
surely by coupling of the initial distributions. Since $e^{-s\lambda(t-\tau)} \leq 1$ 
for all $\tau \leq t$, we can bound on each path
\begin{align*}
    \left\|{F^i_t}\right\| \leq s\lambda \int_0^t \left\|{D_\tau}\right\|\, d\tau 
    \leq s\lambda \int_0^T \left\|{D_\tau}\right\|\, d\tau,
\end{align*}
We fix ${\Tilde{T}} \in [0, T]$ and take the $p$th power and supremum over $t \in [0,{\Tilde{T}}]$:
\begin{align*}
    \underset{t \in [0, \Tilde{T}]}{\sup} \left\|{F^i_t}\right\|^p 
    \leq \bigg( s\lambda \int_0^{\Tilde{T}} \left\|{D_\tau}\right\|\, d\tau \bigg)^p 
    \leq (s\lambda)^p {\Tilde{T}}^{p-1} \int_0^{\Tilde{T}} \left\|{D_\tau}\right\|^p\, d\tau.
\end{align*}
where we applied Hölder's inequality in the last step. Multiplying by $\mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}$, taking 
expectations and applying the Fubini-Tonelli theorem yields
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E}\bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, \Tilde{T}]}{\sup} \left\|{F^i_t}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg] 
    \leq (s\lambda)^p {\Tilde{T}}^{p-1} \int_0^{\Tilde{T}} 
    \mathbb{E}\big[ \left\|{D_\tau}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \big]\, d\tau. \label{RestrictedDelta Bound1}
\end{align}
We now expand $\left\|{D_\tau}\right\|^p$ by inserting the empirical 
mean-field measure $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_\tau$ and applying 
the inequality $\left\|{a + b}\right\|^p \leq 2^{p-1}\Bigg( \left\|{a}\right\|^p + \left\|{b}\right\|^p \Bigg)$:
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E}\big[ \left\|{D_\tau}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \big] 
    &= \mathbb{E}\Big[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_\tau) 
    - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_\tau)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \Big] \\
    &\leq 2^{p-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_\tau) 
    - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_\tau)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \Big] 
    + 2^{p-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_\tau) 
    - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_\tau)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \Big].
\end{align*}
We recognize that on $\Omega_M$, we can apply Lemma \ref{CarrL2} and 
Example \ref{WasserBD} alongside with the exchangeability of the particles to the first term:
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E}\Big[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_\tau) 
    - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_\tau)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \Big] 
    &\leq C_0^p\, \mathbb{E}\Big[ W_p^p(\rho^N_\tau, 
    \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_\tau)\mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \Big] \notag \\
    &\leq C_0^p\, \mathbb{E}\bigg[  \frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{j=1}\left\|{X^j_\tau 
    - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^j_\tau}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg] \notag \\
    &= C_0^p\, \mathbb{E}\bigg[ \left\|{X^i_\tau 
    - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_\tau}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg] \notag \\
    &\leq C_0^p\, \mathbb{E}\bigg[ \underset{s \in [0,\tau]}
    {\sup}\left\|{F^i_s}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg]. \label{Restricted Delta Est1}
\end{align}
For every $\tau \in [0,T]$, define
\begin{align*}
\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\Omega}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\Omega}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\Omega_{M,\tau}
:=
\left\{
\omega \in \Omega :
\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^N \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_\tau}\right\|^p \le M
\right\}.
\end{align*}
Since
\begin{align*}
\Omega_M \subseteq
\left\{
\omega \in \Omega :
\sup_{t \in [0,T]} \frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^N \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \le M
\right\}
\subseteq \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\Omega}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\Omega}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\Omega_{M,\tau},
\end{align*}
we have
\begin{align*}
\mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \le \mathbbm{1}_{\overline{\Omega}_{M,\tau}}.
\end{align*}
Therefore, in the second term, we can invoke Lemma \ref{CH:ConsBound}:
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E}\Big[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_\tau) 
    - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_\tau)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \Big] \leq \mathbb{E}\Big[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_\tau) 
    - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_\tau)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\overline{\Omega}_{M, \tau}} \Big]
    \leq C_{\mathrm{EM}} N^{-\frac{p}{2}}. \label{Restricted Delta Est2}
\end{align}
Combining \eqref{Restricted Delta Est1} and \eqref{Restricted Delta Est2} gives:
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E}\big[ \left\|{D_\tau}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \big] 
    \leq 2^{p-1} C_0^p\, 
    \mathbb{E}\bigg[ \underset{s \in [0,\tau]}{\sup}
    \left\|{F^i_s}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg] 
    + 2^{p-1} C_{\mathrm{EM}} N^{-\frac{p}{2}}.
\end{align*}
Putting the latter into \eqref{RestrictedDelta Bound1} yields
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E}\bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, \Tilde{T}]}{\sup} \left\|{F^i_t}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg] 
    &\leq (s\lambda)^p {\Tilde{T}}^{p-1} \int_0^{\Tilde{T}} 
    \bigg( 2^{p-1} C_0^p\, 
    \mathbb{E}\bigg[ \underset{s \in [0,\tau]}{\sup}
    \left\|{F^i_s}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg] 
    + 2^{p-1} C_{\mathrm{EM}} N^{-\frac{p}{2}} \bigg) d\tau \\
    &= (2{\Tilde{T}})^{p-1}(s\lambda C_0)^p  
    \int_0^{\Tilde{T}} \mathbb{E}\bigg[ \underset{s \in [0,\tau]}{\sup}
    \left\|{F^i_s}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg]\, d\tau 
    + 2^{p-1}(s\lambda {\Tilde{T}})^p C_{\mathrm{EM}} N^{-\frac{p}{2}}.
\end{align*}
The above bound allows us to apply Grönwall's 
Lemma (Lemma \ref{GronwallLemma}) to ultimately get
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E}\bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup}\left\|{X^i_t - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg] \leq 2^{p-1}(s\lambda T)^p C_{\mathrm{EM}} N^{-\frac{p}{2}} e^{2^{p-1}(s\lambda T C_0)^p} =: \varepsilon^{\mathrm{rest}}_{\mathrm{D}}(N, T).
\end{align*}
Finally, if $\mathbb{P}(\Omega_M)>0$, then
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E}\bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup}\left\|{X^i_t - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \Big| \Omega_M \bigg]
    =
    \frac{\mathbb{E}\bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup}\left\|{X^i_t - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg]}{\mathbb{P}(\Omega_M)}
    \leq \frac{\varepsilon^{\mathrm{rest}}_{\mathrm{D}}(N,T)}{\mathbb{P}(\Omega_M)}.
\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- remark-7: body_md -->
Note that this rate is independent of the exponent $p$. As long as the initial distribution has finite $p$th moments, i.e. $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_0 \in \mathcal P_p(\mathbb R^d)$ with $p\ge 2$, we obtain propagation of chaos in Wasserstein-$p$ distance at rate $\mathcal O(N^{-1/2})$.
<!-- end remark-7 -->
<!-- lemma-12: body_md -->
Consider the particle system $\mathcal{X}^N_{T}$ and $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\mathcal{X}^N_{T}$ defined by \eqref{CF:CFscheme} and \eqref{CF:MFIPS}, respectively, with initial data following the distribution $\rho_0 \in \mathcal{P}_p(\mathbb{R}^d)$ and let $J$ be the number of freezing intervals such that $[0, T] = \bigcup_{0 \leq j \leq J-1} [t_j, t_{j+1}]$. Then there exist constants $C_{\mathrm{F}} >0 $ and $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{C}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}C_{\mathrm{F}} >0 $ independent of $N$ such that for every $N \in \mathbb{N}$ and every $i \leq N$
    \begin{align*}
        \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{0 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_{t_j}}\right\|^p \bigg] \leq C_{\mathrm{F}} \quad \text{and} \quad \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{0 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{t_j}}\right\|^p \bigg] \leq \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{C}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}C_{\mathrm{F}}.
    \end{align*}
    \label{NP:CFLemma}
<!-- end lemma-12 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
We will prove the statement just for the particle system $\mathcal{X}^N_{T}$. The corresponding result for $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\mathcal{X}^N_{T}$ follows the same line of argument.

    Fix $N \in \mathbb{N}$, $i \leq N, j < J$ and consider the time interval $[t_j, t_{j+1})$ and assume an initial condition $X^i_{t_j}$. Then we can clearly see that 
    \begin{align*}
         dX^i_t = - s \cdot \lambda (X^i_t - X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_j}))dt + \sqrt{s}\delta dB^i_t,  \quad \forall t \in [t_j, t_{j+1})
    \end{align*}
is an Ornstein-Uhlenbeck process with the strong solution
\begin{align}
    X^i_t = X_{t_j}^i e^{- s \lambda (t-t_j)} + X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_j})(1- e^{-s \lambda (t-t_j)}) +  \sqrt{s}\delta\int^t_{t_j} e^{-s \lambda (t- \tau)} dB^i_\tau.
    \label{OU:Strong}
\end{align}
Therefore, at each interval endpoint $t_j$, the process is determined as follows
\begin{align}
    X^i_{t_j} &= X^i_{t_{j-1}} e^{-s\lambda (t_j - t_{j-1})} + X^{\alpha}(\rho^N_{t_{j-1}})(1- e^{-s\lambda (t_j - t_{j-1})}) + \sqrt{s} \delta \int^{t_j}_{t_{j-1}} e^{-s\lambda (t_j - \tau)}dB_\tau^i.
    \label{OUStrong2}
\end{align}
For every $j \in \{1, ..., J \}$, we define 
\begin{align}
    Z^i_j := \int^{t_j}_{t_{j-1}} e^{-s\lambda (t_j - \tau)}dB_\tau^i.
    \label{CFMomentZ_jDefinition}
\end{align}
Note that $Z^i_j$ is a Brownian motion integral, with a square integrable integrand over its time horizon. This means that $Z^i_j$ is a Gaussian random vector with zero mean and variance
\begin{align*}
     \mathrm{Cov}(Z_j^i)
    = \mathbb{E}\big[ Z_j^i (Z_j^i)^\top \big] 
    &= \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ 
    \bigg( \int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_j} e^{-s\lambda (t_j-\tau)}\, dB_\tau^i \bigg)
    \bigg( \int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_j} e^{-s\lambda (t_j-\tau)}\, dB_\tau^i \bigg)^\top
     \Bigg]\\
    &= \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ 
    \bigg( \int_{0}^{\Delta t} e^{-s\lambda (\Delta t -\tau)}\, dB_\tau^i \bigg)
    \bigg( \int_{0}^{\Delta t} e^{-s\lambda (\Delta t -\tau)}\, dB_\tau^i \bigg)^\top
     \Bigg].
\end{align*}
By time-homogeneity and Itô isometry, this becomes
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ 
    \bigg( \int_{0}^{\Delta t} e^{-s\lambda (\Delta t -\tau)}\, dB_\tau^i \bigg)
    \bigg( \int_{0}^{\Delta t} e^{-s\lambda (\Delta t -\tau)}\, dB_\tau^i \bigg)^\top
     \Bigg]
    = \int_0^{\Delta t} e^{-2s\lambda(\Delta t-\tau)}\, d\tau \, I_d
    = \frac{1-e^{-2s\lambda \Delta t}}{2s\lambda}\, I_d.
\end{align*}
Therefore,
\[
Z_j^i \sim \mathcal N\bigg(0,\frac{1-e^{-2s\lambda \Delta t}}{2s\lambda}\, I_d\bigg).
\]
Let us now shift our attention to our actual goal which is bounding the moments uniformly. We begin by rewriting \eqref{OUStrong2} using the definition of $Z^i_j$ and the fact that $t_j - t_{j-1} = \Delta t$:
\begin{align}
    X^i_{t_j} = X^i_{t_{j-1}} e^{-s\lambda \Delta t} + X^{\alpha}(\rho^N_{t_{j-1}})(1- e^{-s\lambda \Delta t}) + \sqrt{s} \delta Z^i_j.
    \label{OUStrong3}
\end{align}
We use \eqref{OUStrong3}, which holds for $j \geq 1$, to derive a straightforward first bound, treating the initial time separately:
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{0 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_{t_j}}\right\|^p \bigg] &\leq \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{X^i_0}\right\|^p \Big] + \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{1 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_{t_{j-1}} e^{-s\lambda \Delta t} + X^{\alpha}(\rho^N_{t_{j-1}})(1- e^{-s\lambda \Delta t}) + \sqrt{s} \delta Z^i_j}\right\|^p \bigg] \notag \\
    &\leq \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{X^i_0}\right\|^p \Big] + 2^{p-1} \Bigg( \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{1 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_{t_{j-1}} e^{-s\lambda \Delta t} + X^{\alpha}(\rho^N_{t_{j-1}})(1- e^{-s\lambda \Delta t})}\right\|^p  \bigg]\notag  \\ & \quad + \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{1 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{\sqrt{s} \delta Z^i_j}\right\|^p \bigg] \Bigg).
    \label{CF:NPBoundHopping}
\end{align}
We begin by treating the first expectation. Since $e^{-s\lambda \Delta t} \in (0,1)$ and the map
$x \mapsto \left\|{x}\right\|^p$ is convex, we can estimate
\begin{align}
    &\mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{1 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_{t_{j-1}} e^{-s\lambda \Delta t} + X^{\alpha}(\rho^N_{t_{j-1}})(1- e^{-s\lambda \Delta t})}\right\|^p  \bigg] \notag \\
    &\leq
    \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ 
    \underset{1 \leq j \leq J}{\sup}
    \bigg( 
    e^{-s\lambda \Delta t}\left\|{X^i_{t_{j-1}}}\right\|^p
    +
    (1- e^{-s\lambda \Delta t})\left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_{j-1}})}\right\|^p
     \bigg) \Bigg]
    \notag \\
    &\leq
    e^{-s\lambda \Delta t}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{1 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_{t_{j-1}}}\right\|^p \bigg]
    +
    (1- e^{-s\lambda \Delta t})
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{1 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{X^{\alpha}(\rho^N_{t_{j-1}})}\right\|^p \bigg].
    \label{FrMoment:1stExpbound1}
\end{align}
We now write $\mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{1 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{X^{\alpha}(\rho^N_{t_{j-1}})}\right\|^p \bigg]$ in terms of $\mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{1 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_{t_{j-1}}}\right\|^p \bigg]$. \newline
We start by computing
\begin{align}
   \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{1 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_{j-1}})}\right\|^p \bigg] = \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \underset{1 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{\int \frac{x \omega_\alpha(x)}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\rho^N_{t_{j-1}}})}} d\rho^N_{t_{j-1}}(x)}\right\|^p \Bigg]. \label{FrMomentConsensus}
\end{align}
We define the random measure $\eta^\alpha_{t_{j-1}} := \frac{\omega_\alpha \rho^N_{t_{j-1}}}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\rho^N_{t_{j-1}}})}}$\footnote{$\eta^\alpha_{t_{j-1}}$ is measurable in the sense of Proposition \ref{Pre:PropEmpMeas}} which is a probability measure on the paths on which $\rho^N_{t_{j-1}}$ is a probability measure. Therefore, with Jensen's inequality, we can estimate:
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \underset{1 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{\int \frac{x \omega_\alpha(x)}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\rho^N_{t_{j-1}}})}} d\rho^N_{t_{j-1}}(x)}\right\|^p \Bigg] &= \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \underset{1 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{\int x \,  d\eta^\alpha_{t_{j-1}}(x)}\right\|^p \Bigg] \notag \\ &\leq \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \underset{1 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \int \left\|{x}\right\|^p \,  d\eta^\alpha_{t_{j-1}}(x) \Bigg]. \label{FrMomentBound1}
\end{align}
With Lemma \ref{GerberPropA4}, we get a constant $b_p>0$ such that
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \underset{1 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \int \left\|{x}\right\|^p \,  d\eta^\alpha_{t_{j-1}}(x) \Bigg] =  \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \underset{1 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \,  \mathbb{E}_{\eta^\alpha_{t_{j-1}}} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big] \Bigg] \leq \mathbb{E} \bigg[ b_p \, \underset{1 \leq j \leq J}{\sup}\, \mathbb{E}_{\rho^N_{t_{j-1}}} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big] \bigg]. \label{FrMomentBound2}
\end{align}
We use \eqref{FrMomentBound1} and \eqref{FrMomentBound2} to obtain for \eqref{FrMomentConsensus}
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{0 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_{j-1}})}\right\|^p \bigg] \leq \mathbb{E} \bigg[ b_p\,  \underset{0 \leq j \leq J}{\sup}\, \mathbb{E}_{\rho^N_{t_{j-1}}} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big] \bigg] = b_p \, \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{0 \leq j \leq J}{\sup}\, \mathbb{E}_{\rho^N_{t_{j-1}}} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big] \bigg].
    \label{NP:FrMoment2}
\end{align}
Writing out the expectation with respect to $\rho^N_{t_{j-1}}$ yields
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{1 \leq j \leq J}{\sup}\, \mathbb{E}_{\rho^N_{t_{j-1}}} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big] \bigg]
    = \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{1 \leq j \leq J}{\sup}\, \frac{1}{N}\sum^N_{m=1} \left\|{X^m_{t_{j-1}}}\right\|^p \bigg]
    &\leq \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \frac{1}{N}\sum^N_{m=1} \underset{1 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \,  \left\|{X^m_{t_{j-1}}}\right\|^p \bigg] \\
    &= \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{0 \leq j \leq J-1}{\sup} \,  \left\|{X^i_{t_{j}}}\right\|^p \bigg], 
\end{align*}
where we used the invariance of the expectation under permutations of the particle index in the last equality.
We combine the latter with \eqref{NP:FrMoment2} to get our estimate for the expected $p$th moment of the consensus point.
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{1 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_{j-1}})}\right\|^p \bigg] \leq b_p \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{0 \leq j \leq J-1}{\sup} \,  \left\|{X^i_{t_{j}}}\right\|^p \bigg].
\end{align*}
Now, we can use the latter on our estimate \eqref{FrMoment:1stExpbound1} to further compute
\begin{align}
    &\mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{1 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_{t_{j-1}} e^{-s\lambda \Delta t} + X^{\alpha}(\rho^N_{t_{j-1}})(1- e^{-s\lambda \Delta t})}\right\|^p  \bigg] \notag \\
    &\leq e^{-s\lambda \Delta t}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{1 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_{t_{j-1}}}\right\|^p \bigg]+(1- e^{-s\lambda \Delta t}) \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{1 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{X^{\alpha}(\rho^N_{t_{j-1}})}\right\|^p \bigg] \notag \\
    &\leq \bigg( e^{-s\lambda \Delta t}+ b_p(1- e^{-s\lambda \Delta t}) \bigg) \mathbb{E} \bigg[   \underset{0 \leq j \leq J-1}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_{t_{j}}}\right\|^p \bigg].
\end{align}
We combine the latter with \eqref{CF:NPBoundHopping} and set
\begin{align*}
    a_s := 2^{p-1}\Big( e^{-s\lambda \Delta t}+ b_p(1- e^{-s\lambda \Delta t}) \Big)
\end{align*}
to get the estimate
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{0 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_{t_j}}\right\|^p \bigg]
    \leq \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{X^i_0}\right\|^p \Big] + a_s\, \mathbb{E} \bigg[   \underset{0 \leq j \leq J-1}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_{t_{j}}}\right\|^p \bigg] + 2^{p-1} s^{\frac{p}{2}}\delta^p\, \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{1 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{Z^i_j}\right\|^p \bigg].
\end{align*}
Since this estimate holds for every number of freezing intervals, we can iterate it and obtain
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{0 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_{t_j}}\right\|^p \bigg]
    \leq \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{X^i_0}\right\|^p \Big] \sum^J_{l=0} a_s^l
    + 2^{p-1} s^{\frac{p}{2}}\delta^p \sum^{J}_{l = 1} a_s^{J-l}\, \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{1 \leq j \leq l}{\sup} \left\|{Z^i_j}\right\|^p \bigg].
\end{align*}
Since $Z^i_1, ..., Z^i_J$ are i.i.d.\ normal random vectors with zero mean, we can use Lemma \ref{Pre:LemmaNormalVectors} to get a constant $C_p >0$ depending only on $p$ such that for every $l \leq J$
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{1 \leq j \leq l}{\sup} \left\|{Z^i_j}\right\|^p \bigg] \leq \bigg( \frac{1- e^{- 2 s \lambda \Delta t}}{2s \lambda} \bigg)^{\frac{p}{2}} C_p \Big( d\log (2ld) \Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}.
\end{align*}
This leads to our final estimate
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{0 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_{t_j}}\right\|^p \bigg]
    &\leq \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^i_0}\right\|^p \Big] \sum^J_{l=0} a_s^l
    + 2^{p-1}\delta^p \bigg( \frac{1- e^{- 2s\lambda \Delta t}}{2 \lambda} \bigg)^{\frac{p}{2}} C_p \sum^{J}_{l = 1} \Big( d\log (2ld) \Big)^{\frac{p}{2}} a_s^{J-l}\\
    &=: C_{\mathrm{F}}(s).
\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- lemma-13: body_md -->
Let $T >0$ be a fixed final time, and let the interval $[0, T]$ be divided by $0= t_0 < t_1 <...<t_J = T$ with uniform step size $\Delta t$. Let $\big( \mathcal{X}^N_t \big)_{t \in [0, T]} \text{ and } \big( \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\mathcal{X}^N_t \big)_{t \in [0, T]}$ be defined by \eqref{CF:CFscheme} and \eqref{CF:MFIPS}, respectively, with initial data following the distribution $\rho_0 \in \mathcal{P}_p(\mathbb{R}^d)$. For a fixed \(M>0\), define the set
   \begin{align*}
       \Omega_M := \bigg\{ \omega \in \Omega : \underset{0 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \,  \frac{1}{N} \sum_{i=1}^{N} \max \Big\{ \left\|{X^i_{t_j}}\right\|^p , \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{t_j}}\right\|^p \Big \} \leq M \bigg\}.
   \end{align*} Then there exists a constant $C>0$ independent of $N$ such that
    \begin{align*}
        \mathbb{P}(\Omega_M) \geq 1 - \frac{C}{M}.
    \end{align*}
    \label{MFF:Moment}
<!-- end lemma-13 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
In the same way as in Lemma \ref{MFD:Moment}, we obtain with Markov's inequality 
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{P}(\Omega^c_M) \leq \frac{\underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max}\, \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{0 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_{t_j}}\right\|^p  \bigg]}{M} + \frac{\underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max}\, \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{0 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{ \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{t_j}}\right\|^p \bigg] }{M}.
\end{align*}
We use Lemma \ref{NP:CFLemma} to obtain 
\begin{align*}
    \underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max}\, \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{0 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_{t_j}}\right\|^p  \bigg] \leq C_{\mathrm{F}} \quad \text{and} \quad \underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max}\, \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{0 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{t_j}}\right\|^p  \bigg] \leq \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{C}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}C_{\mathrm{F}}.
\end{align*}
We set $\frac{C}{2} \coloneqq \max \Big \{ C_{\mathrm{F}}, \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{C}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}C_{\mathrm{F}} \Big \}$ and get
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max}\, \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{0 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_{t_j}}\right\|^p  \bigg]}{M} + \frac{\underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max}\, \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{0 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{ \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{t_j}}\right\|^p  \bigg]}{M} \leq \frac{C}{M}.
\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
Let $T >0$ be a fixed final time, and let the interval $[0, T]$ be divided by $0= t_0 < t_1 <...<t_J = T$ with uniform step size $\Delta t$. Let $\mathcal{X}^N_T \text{ and } \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\mathcal{X}^N_T$ be defined by \eqref{CF:CFscheme} and \eqref{CF:MFIPS}, respectively, both sharing the same initial data $\mathcal{X}^N_0 = \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\mathcal{X}^N_0$ almost surely, with common distribution $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_0 \in \mathcal{P}_p(\mathbb{R}^d)$ for some $p \geq 2$. For a fixed \(M>0\), define the set
    \begin{align*}
     \Omega_M := \bigg\{ \omega \in \Omega : \underset{0 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \,  \frac{1}{N} \sum_{i=1}^{N} \max \Big\{ \left\|{X^i_{t_j}}\right\|^p , \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{t_j}}\right\|^p \Big \} \leq M \bigg\}.
 \end{align*}
    Then there exists a $\varepsilon^{\mathrm{rest}}_{\mathrm{F}}(N, T) >0$ with $\varepsilon^{\mathrm{rest}}_{\mathrm{F}}(N, T) \xrightarrow[]{N \rightarrow \infty}0$ such that for every $N \in \mathbb{N}$ and every $i \leq N$
    \begin{align*}
           \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \,\left\|{ X^i_t - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \bigg] \leq \varepsilon^{\mathrm{rest}}_{\mathrm{F}}(N, T).
    \end{align*}
    Consequently, if $\mathbb{P}(\Omega_M)>0$, then for every $N \in \mathbb{N}$ and every $i \leq N$
    \begin{align*}
           \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \,\left\|{ X^i_t - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \Big| \Omega_M  \bigg] \leq \frac{\varepsilon^{\mathrm{rest}}_{\mathrm{F}}(N,T)}{\mathbb{P}(\Omega_M)}.
    \end{align*}
    \label{MFF: Thrm}
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
Let $j \in \{0, ..., J-1 \}$ be an arbitrary index and fix $i \leq N$. Our strategy is to first find an estimate for the term 
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{t \in [t_j, t_{j+1})}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_{t} - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{t} }\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \bigg],
\end{align*}
deduce from that a bound over the whole time horizon for
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_{t} - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{t} }\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \bigg].
\end{align*}
Before we prove the actual statement, we need to clarify some things about the supremum of the difference term. The crucial simplification in the freezing scheme is that, within each interval $[t_j,t_{j+1})$, the two coupled particles evolve with frozen consensus points. Hence, the pathwise error on the whole interval is reduced to the error at the interval endpoints. We recall the function $F^{(i)}(t)$ \eqref{CF:DistFunction} that satisfies
\begin{align*}
   X^i_t - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t =  F^{(i)}(t) = D_j + (F^{(i)}(t_j) - D_j)e^{-s \lambda(t-t_j)} \quad \forall t \in [t_j, t_{j+1}). 
\end{align*}
With slight abuse of the notation, we compute without writing expectations down:
\begin{align*}
\underset{t \in [t_j, t_{j+1})}{\sup} \left\|{F^{(i)}(t)}\right\| &= \underset{t \in [t_j, t_{j+1})}{\sup} \left\|{D_j + (F^{(i)}(t_j) - D_j)e^{-s \lambda(t-t_j)}}\right\|\\ &= \underset{t \in [0, \Delta t)}{\sup}  \left\|{D_j + (F^{(i)}(t_j) - D_j)e^{-s \lambda t}}\right\| \\
&= \underset{t \in [e^{- s \lambda\Delta t}, 1]}{\sup}  \left\|{D_j + (F^{(i)}(t_j) - D_j)t}\right\| \\ &= \underset{t \in [e^{- s \lambda \Delta t}, 1]}{\sup}  \left\|{tF^{(i)}(t_j) + (1-t) D_j}\right\|.
\end{align*}
We define $h \colon [e^{- s \lambda\Delta t}, 1] \rightarrow \mathbb{R}^d, \quad t \mapsto tF^{(i)}(t_j) + (1-t) D_j$. This is a line segment between $F^{(i)}(t_j)$ and $D_j$. Together with the convexity of the norms, this results in $t \mapsto \left\|{h(t)}\right\|$ being convex. Therefore, on the closed interval $[e^{-s \lambda\Delta t}, 1]$, as a continuous convex function, $t \mapsto \left\|{h(t)}\right\|$ attains its maximum at the endpoints, i.e.
\begin{align*}
    \underset{t \in [e^{- s \lambda\Delta t}, 1]}{\sup}  \left\|{tF^{(i)}(t_j) + (1-t) D_j}\right\|  = \max \Big \{ \left\|{F^{(i)}(t_j)}\right\|, \left\|{e^{-s \lambda \Delta t}F^{(i)}(t_j) + (1-e^{-s \lambda\Delta t}) D_j}\right\| \Big \}
\end{align*}
This means that
\begin{align}
    &\mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{t \in [t_j, t_{j+1})}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_{t} - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{t} }\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \bigg] \notag \\ = \, &   \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \max \Big \{ \left\|{F^{(i)}(t_j)}\right\|^p, \left\|{e^{-s \lambda\Delta t}F^{(i)}(t_j) + (1-e^{-s \lambda\Delta t}) D_j}\right\|^p \Big \} \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \bigg] \notag \\
     = \, &\mathbb{E} \bigg[ \max \Big \{ \left\|{E^{(i)}_j}\right\|^p, \left\|{E^{(i)}_{j+1}}\right\|^p \Big \} \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \bigg], \notag \\
     \label{CF:2Cases}
\end{align}
where $E^{(i)}_j$ is defined in (\ref{MFF:DistanceScheme}).
We deduce for every $j \in \{0,\dots,J-1\}$ that
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{t \in [t_j, t_{j+1})}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_{t} - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{t} }\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \bigg]
    &=
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \max \Big \{ \left\|{E^{(i)}_j}\right\|^p, \left\|{E^{(i)}_{j+1}}\right\|^p \Big \} \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \bigg] \notag\\
    &\leq
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \Big(\left\|{E^{(i)}_j}\right\|^p + \left\|{E^{(i)}_{j+1}}\right\|^p\Big)\mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg].
    \label{FreezingRestrIntervalSupBound}
\end{align}
Hence it is enough to estimate the quantities
\begin{align*}
A_j := \mathbb{E}\bigg[ \left\|{E^{(i)}_j}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg], \qquad j=0,\dots,J.
\end{align*}
Since $E^{(i)}_0 = 0$, we have
\begin{align*}
A_0 = 0.
\end{align*}
We notice that $e^{-s\lambda \Delta t} \in (0, 1)$ and that $x \mapsto \left\|{x}\right\|^p$ is convex.
So for fixed $j \leq J-1$ and $i \leq N$, 
\begin{align}
    A_{j+1}
    &= \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{gE^{(i)}_j + D_j(1-g)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \bigg] \notag\\
    &\leq g \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \left\|{E_j^{(i)}}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \bigg]
    + (1-g) \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{D_j}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \Big].
    \label{FreezingRestrTheoremBound1'}
\end{align}
We now proceed to make a suitable bound for $\mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{D_j}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \Big]$.
As before,
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{D_j}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \Big]
    &=
    \mathbb{E}\bigg[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_j}) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_{t_j})}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg] \notag\\
    &\leq 2^{p-1}\Bigg( \mathbb{E}\bigg[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_j}) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_{t_j})}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg]
    +\mathbb{E} \bigg[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_{t_j}) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_{t_j})}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg] \Bigg).
    \label{FreezingRestrTheoremBound2'}
\end{align}
On the left hand side of the sum, we invoke Lemma \ref{CarrL2}, Example \ref{WasserBD}, and exchangeability:
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E}\bigg[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_j}) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_{t_j})}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg]
    &\leq C_0^p \mathbb{E}\bigg[  W_p^p(\rho^N_{t_j}, \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_{t_j}) \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg]\\
    &\leq C_0^p \mathbb{E}\bigg[ \frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i=1}\left\|{X^i_{t_j} - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{t_j}}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg]\\
    &= C_0^p \mathbb{E}\bigg[  \left\|{X^i_{t_j} - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{t_j}}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg]
    \\
    &= C_0^p A_j.
\end{align*}
As before, the right-hand side of \eqref{FreezingRestrTheoremBound2'} is bounded by Lemma \ref{CH:ConsBound}:
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_{t_j}) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_{t_j})}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg]
    \leq C_{\mathrm{EM}} N^{-\frac{p}{2}}.
\end{align*}
Therefore,
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{D_j}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \Big]
    \leq 2^{p-1}\Bigg( C_0^p A_j + C_{\mathrm{EM}} N^{-\frac{p}{2}} \Bigg).
\end{align*}
Putting this into \eqref{FreezingRestrTheoremBound1'} yields
\begin{align}
    A_{j+1}
    &\leq
    \big( g + 2^{p-1}(1-g) C_0^p \big) A_j
    + 2^{p-1}(1-g) C_{\mathrm{EM}} N^{-\frac{p}{2}}.
    \label{FreezingRestrRecursion}
\end{align}
Define
\begin{align*}
a_F := g + 2^{p-1}(1-g) C_0^p,
\qquad
b_F := 2^{p-1}(1-g) C_{\mathrm{EM}}.
\end{align*}
Then \eqref{FreezingRestrRecursion} becomes
\begin{align*}
A_{j+1} \leq a_F A_j + b_F N^{-p/2}.
\end{align*}
Since $A_0=0$, a straightforward induction gives
\begin{align}
    A_j
    \leq b_F N^{-\frac{p}{2}} \sum_{l=0}^{j-1} a_F^l,
    \qquad j=1,\dots,J.
    \label{FreezingRestrExplicitAj}
\end{align}
Using \eqref{FreezingRestrIntervalSupBound}, we get for each $j=0,\dots,J-1$
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{t \in [t_j, t_{j+1})}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_{t} - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{t} }\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \bigg]
    \leq A_j + A_{j+1}.
\end{align*}
Hence
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \,\left\|{ X^i_t - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \bigg]
    &\leq \sum_{j=0}^{J-1} \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{t \in [t_j, t_{j+1})}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_{t} - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{t} }\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \bigg] \\
    &\leq \sum_{j=0}^{J-1}(A_j + A_{j+1})
    \leq 2\sum_{j=1}^{J} A_j \\
    &\leq 2 b_F N^{-\frac{p}{2}} \sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{l=0}^{j-1} a_F^l \\
    &= 2 b_F N^{-\frac{p}{2}} \sum_{l=0}^{J-1} (J-l) a_F^l
    =: \varepsilon^{\mathrm{rest}}_{\mathrm{F}}(N,T).
\end{align*}
Thus
\begin{align}
    \varepsilon^{\mathrm{rest}}_{\mathrm{F}}(N,T)
    =
    2\cdot 2^{p-1}(1-g) C_{\mathrm{EM}}
    N^{-\frac{p}{2}} \sum_{l=0}^{J-1}(J-l)
    \Big(g + 2^{p-1}(1-g) C_0^p\Big)^l
    .
    \label{FreezingRestrExplicitEpsilon}
\end{align}
Finally, if $\mathbb{P}(\Omega_M)>0$, then by conditional expectation,
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \,\left\|{ X^i_t - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \Big| \Omega_M  \bigg]
    =
    \frac{\mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \,\left\|{ X^i_t - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \bigg]}{\mathbb{P}(\Omega_M)}
    \leq \frac{\varepsilon^{\mathrm{rest}}_{\mathrm{F}}(N,T)}{\mathbb{P}(\Omega_M)}.
\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- lemma-14: body_md -->
Consider the particle systems $\mathcal{X}^N_{K}$ and $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\mathcal{X}^N_{K}$ defined by \eqref{CH:Scheme} and \eqref{CH:MFScheme}, respectively, with initial data following the distribution $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_0 \in \mathcal{P}_p(\mathbb{R}^d)$ and a final time $K \in \mathbb{N}_{>0}$. Then there exist constants $C_{\mathrm{H}} >0$ and $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{C}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}C_{\mathrm{H}} >0$, both independent of $N$, such that for every $N \in \mathbb{N}$ and every $i \leq N$
    \begin{align*}
        \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K}}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_k}\right\|^p \bigg] \leq C_{\mathrm{H}} \quad \text{and} \quad \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K}}{\sup} \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_k}\right\|^p \bigg] \leq \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{C}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}C_{\mathrm{H}}.
    \end{align*}
    \label{NP_CHLemma}
<!-- end lemma-14 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
We show the proof only for the hopping particle system \eqref{CH:Scheme}. The proof for \eqref{CH:MFScheme} follows the same line of argument.
We fix $i \leq N$. Since $X^i_k = X^\alpha(\rho^N_{k-1}) + \eta^i_k$ holds only for $k \geq 1$, we separate the initial time and use the definition of the hopping scheme \eqref{CH:Scheme}:
    \begin{align}
        \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K}}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_k}\right\|^p \bigg]
        &\leq \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{X^i_0}\right\|^p \Big] + \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \sup_{1 \leq k \leq K} \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{k-1}) + \eta^i_k}\right\|^p \bigg] \notag \\
