        &\leq \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{X^i_0}\right\|^p \Big] + 2^{p-1} \bigg(  \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \sup_{1 \leq k \leq K} \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{k-1})}\right\|^p \bigg] + \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \sup_{1 \leq k \leq K} \left\|{\eta^i_k}\right\|^p \bigg] \bigg).
        \label{NP:Hopping_Moment1}
    \end{align}
The second term on the right hand side is straightforward to estimate, as it is the expected supremum of i.i.d. Gaussian random vectors, for which we can use Lemma \ref{Pre:LemmaNormalVectors}. 

We now turn to the first term. We compute
\begin{align}
   \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \sup_{1 \leq k \leq K} \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{k-1})}\right\|^p \bigg]
   = \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \sup_{1 \leq k \leq K} \left\|{\int \frac{x \omega_\alpha(x)}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\rho^N_{k-1}})}} \, d\rho^N_{k-1}(x)}\right\|^p \Bigg].
   \label{HoppingMomentConsensus}
\end{align}
We define the random measure
\begin{align*}
\eta^\alpha_{k-1} := \frac{\omega_\alpha \rho^N_{k-1}}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\rho^N_{k-1}})}},
\end{align*}
which is measurable by Proposition \ref{Pre:PropEmpMeas}. Since $\eta^\alpha_{k-1}$ is a probability measure whenever $\rho^N_{k-1}$ is a probability measure, Jensen's inequality yields
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \sup_{1 \leq k \leq K} \left\|{\int \frac{x \omega_\alpha(x)}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\rho^N_{k-1}})}} d\rho^N_{k-1}(x)}\right\|^p \Bigg]
    &= \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \sup_{1 \leq k \leq K} \left\|{\int x \, d\eta^\alpha_{k-1}(x)}\right\|^p \Bigg] \notag \\
    &\leq \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \sup_{1 \leq k \leq K} \int \left\|{x}\right\|^p \, d\eta^\alpha_{k-1}(x) \Bigg].
    \label{HoppingMomentBound1}
\end{align}
Using Lemma \ref{GerberPropA4} with exponent $p$, we obtain
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \sup_{1 \leq k \leq K} \int \left\|{x}\right\|^p \, d\eta^\alpha_{k-1}(x) \Bigg]
    = \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \sup_{1 \leq k \leq K} \mathbb{E}_{\eta^\alpha_{k-1}} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big] \Bigg]
    \leq \mathbb{E} \bigg[ b_p \, \sup_{1 \leq k \leq K} \mathbb{E}_{\rho^N_{k-1}} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big] \bigg].
    \label{HoppingMomentBound2}
\end{align}
Combining \eqref{HoppingMomentBound1} and \eqref{HoppingMomentBound2}, we get from \eqref{HoppingMomentConsensus}
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \sup_{1 \leq k \leq K} \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{k-1})}\right\|^p \bigg]
    \leq b_p \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \sup_{1 \leq k \leq K}\, \mathbb{E}_{\rho^N_{k-1}} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big] \bigg].
    \label{NP:HoppingMoment2}
\end{align}
Writing out the expectation with respect to $\rho^N_{k-1}$ gives
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \sup_{1 \leq k \leq K}\, \mathbb{E}_{\rho^N_{k-1}} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big] \bigg]
    &= \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \sup_{1 \leq k \leq K}\, \frac{1}{N}\sum^N_{j=1} \left\|{X^j_{k-1}}\right\|^p \bigg] \\
    &\leq \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \frac{1}{N}\sum^N_{j=1} \sup_{1 \leq k \leq K} \left\|{X^j_{k-1}}\right\|^p \bigg] \\
    &= \frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^N \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K-1}}{\sup} \left\|{X^j_k}\right\|^p \bigg] \\
    &= \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K-1}}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_k}\right\|^p \bigg],
\end{align*}
where we used exchangeability of the particles in the last step.
Hence
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \sup_{1 \leq k \leq K} \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{k-1})}\right\|^p \bigg]
    \leq b_p \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K-1}}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_k}\right\|^p \bigg].
\end{align*}
Inserting this into \eqref{NP:Hopping_Moment1}, we arrive at
\begin{align*}
     \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K}}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_k}\right\|^p \bigg]
     \leq \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{X^i_0}\right\|^p \Big] + 2^{p-1} \bigg( b_p \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K-1}}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_k}\right\|^p \bigg] + \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \sup_{1 \leq k \leq K} \left\|{\eta^i_k}\right\|^p \bigg] \bigg).
\end{align*}
Now define
\begin{align*}
A_K := \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K}}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_k}\right\|^p \bigg],
\qquad
B_K := \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \sup_{1 \leq k \leq K} \left\|{\eta^i_k}\right\|^p \bigg],
\end{align*}
so that $A_0 = \mathbb{E}\big[ \left\|{X^i_0}\right\|^p \big]$. Then the previous estimate reads
\begin{align*}
A_K \leq A_0 + 2^{p-1} b_p\, A_{K-1} + 2^{p-1} B_K.
\end{align*}
Iterating this recursion, or equivalently arguing by induction on $K$, yields
\begin{align}
     A_K
     \leq \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{X^i_0}\right\|^p \Big] \sum^K_{l=0} (2^{p-1}b_p)^l
     + 2^{p-1} \sum^K_{l = 1} B_l (2^{p-1}b_p)^{K-l}.
\end{align}
By Lemma \ref{Pre:LemmaNormalVectors} there exists a constant $C_p >0$ depending only on $p$ such that for every $l \leq K$
\begin{align*}
    B_l
    = \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \sup_{1 \leq k \leq l} \left\|{\eta^i_k}\right\|^p \bigg]
    \leq \sigma^pC_p \Big( d\log (2ld) \Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}.
\end{align*}
Therefore,
\begin{align}
     \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K}}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_k}\right\|^p \bigg]
     \leq \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{X^i_0}\right\|^p \Big] \sum^K_{l=0} (2^{p-1}b_p)^l
     + 2^{p-1} \sigma^pC_p \sum^K_{l = 1} \Big( d\log (2ld) \Big)^{\frac{p}{2}} (2^{p-1}b_p)^{K-l}
     =:C_{\mathrm{H}}.
     \label{HoppingMomentFinal estiamte}
\end{align}
We note that $C_{\mathrm{H}}$ is independent of $N$, which concludes the proof.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- remark-8: body_md -->
Recall the consensus freezing moment estimate of Lemma \ref{NP:CFLemma} for $J = K$, with $a_s = 2^{p-1}\big( e^{-s\lambda \Delta t}+ b_p(1- e^{-s\lambda \Delta t}) \big)$, and pass to the limit $s \to \infty$. Since $a_s \to 2^{p-1}b_p$ and $e^{-2s\lambda\Delta t} \to 0$, we obtain
    \begin{align*}
        &\mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{0 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_{t_j}}\right\|^p \bigg]\\
        \leq  \, & \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^i_0}\right\|^p \Big] \sum^K_{l=0} a_s^l
        + 2^{p-1}\delta^p \bigg( \frac{1- e^{- 2s\lambda \Delta t}}{2 \lambda} \bigg)^{\frac{p}{2}} C_p \sum^{K}_{l = 1} \Big( d\log (2ld) \Big)^{\frac{p}{2}} a_s^{K-l} \\
        \xrightarrow[]{s \to \infty }\, & \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{X^i_0}\right\|^p \Big] \sum^K_{l=0} (2^{p-1}b_p)^l
        + 2^{p-1}  C_p \bigg( \frac{\delta}{\sqrt{2 \lambda}} \bigg)^{p}\sum^K_{l = 1}  \Big( d\log (2ld) \Big)^{\frac{p}{2}} (2^{p-1}b_p)^{K-l}.
    \end{align*}
This is exactly the moment estimate \eqref{HoppingMomentFinal estiamte} of the consensus hopping scheme with variance $\sigma^2 = \frac{\delta^2}{2 \lambda}$. This connection aligns with the convergence of the freezing scheme to the hopping scheme for $s \to \infty$, proven in Theorem \ref{thm:FStoHS}, which is stated in Section \ref{section:globalconvergence}.
<!-- end remark-8 -->
<!-- lemma-15: body_md -->
Let $K \in \mathbb{N}$ be the final time. Let $\mathcal{X}^N_K \text{ and } \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\mathcal{X}^N_K$ be the interacting particle systems of the finite particle system (\ref{CH:Scheme}) and the mean-field particle system (\ref{CH:MFScheme}), respectively, with initial data following the distribution $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_0 \in \mathcal{P}_p(\mathbb{R}^d)$. For a fixed \(M>0\), define the set \begin{align*}
       \Omega_M := \bigg\{ \omega \in \Omega : \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K}}{\sup} \frac{1}{N} \sum_{i=1}^{N} \max \Big\{ \left\|{X^i_k}\right\|^p , \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_k}\right\|^p \Big \} \leq M \bigg\}.
   \end{align*}Then there exists a constant $C >0$ independent of $N$ such that
    \begin{align*}
        \mathbb{P}(\Omega_M) \geq 1 - \frac{C}{M}.
    \end{align*}
    \label{CH:Moment}
<!-- end lemma-15 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
As in Lemma \ref{MFD:Moment} and Lemma \ref{MFF:Moment}, we compute with Markov's inequality: 
    \begin{align*}
        \mathbb{P}(\Omega^c_M) \leq \frac{\underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max}\, \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K}}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_k}\right\|^p \bigg ]}{M} + \frac{\underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max}\, \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K}}{\sup} \left\|{ \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_k}\right\|^p  \bigg]}{M}.
    \end{align*}
Lemma \ref{NP_CHLemma} immediately gives us the bounds we need:
\begin{align*}
    \underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max}\, \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K}}{\sup} \left\|{ X^i_k}\right\|^p  \bigg] \leq C_{\mathrm{H}} , \quad \quad \underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max}\, \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K}}{\sup} \left\|{ \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_k}\right\|^p  \bigg] \leq \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{C}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}C_{\mathrm{H}}.
\end{align*}
We set $\frac{C}{2} \coloneqq \max \Big \{ C_{\mathrm{H}}, \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{C}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}C_{\mathrm{H}} \Big \}$ and get
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max}\, \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K}}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_k}\right\|^p \bigg ]}{M} + \frac{\underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max}\, \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K}}{\sup} \left\|{ \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_k}\right\|^p \bigg]}{M} \leq \frac{C}{M}.
\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
Let $K \in \mathbb{N}, N\in \mathbb{N}$. Let $\mathcal{X}^N_K \text{ and } \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\mathcal{X}^N_K$ be the interacting particle systems of the finite particle system (\ref{CH:Scheme}) and the mean-field particle system (\ref{CH:MFScheme}), respectively, both sharing the same initial data $\mathcal{X}^N_0 = \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\mathcal{X}^N_0$ almost surely, with common distribution $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_0 \in \mathcal{P}_p(\mathbb{R}^d)$ for some $p \geq 2$. For a fixed \(M>0\), define
    \begin{align*}
       \Omega_M := \bigg\{ \omega \in \Omega : \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K}}{\sup} \frac{1}{N} \sum_{i=1}^{N} \max \Big\{ \left\|{X^i_k}\right\|^p , \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_k}\right\|^p \Big \} \leq M \bigg\}.
   \end{align*}
    Then there exists a $\varepsilon^{\mathrm{rest}}_{\mathrm{H}}(N, K)>0$ with $\varepsilon^{\mathrm{rest}}_{\mathrm{H}}(N, K) \xrightarrow[]{N \rightarrow \infty}0$ such that for every $N \in \mathbb{N}$ and every $i \leq N$
    \begin{align*}
         \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K}}{\sup} \,\left\|{ X^i_k - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_k}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \bigg] \leq \varepsilon^{\mathrm{rest}}_{\mathrm{H}}(N, K).
    \end{align*}
    Consequently, if $\mathbb{P}(\Omega_M)>0$, then for every $N \in \mathbb{N}$ and every $i \leq N$
    \begin{align*}
         \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K}}{\sup} \,\left\|{ X^i_k - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_k}\right\|^p \Big| \Omega_M  \bigg] \leq \frac{\varepsilon^{\mathrm{rest}}_{\mathrm{H}}(N, K)}{\mathbb{P}(\Omega_M)}.
    \end{align*}
    \label{MFH: Thrm}
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
To start, we bound the expected distance for fixed $i\leq N$ and $k \leq K$:
\begin{align}
   &\mathbb{E} \bigg[  \left\|{X^i_{k+1} - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{k+1}}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg] \notag \\
   = \, &\mathbb{E} \bigg[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_k) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_k)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \bigg] \notag\\
   \leq \, & 2^{p-1} \bigg( \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_k) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg] + \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_k)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg] \bigg).
     \label{HoppingDistEst1}
\end{align}
Here, $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k$ denotes the empirical measure of the mean-field finite particle system at time $k$.
On the first part of this sum, we can invoke Lemma \ref{CarrL2} and then Example \ref{WasserBD} together with the exchangeability of the particles:
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_k) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg]
    \leq C_0^p  \mathbb{E} \Big[ W_p^p(\rho^N_k, \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k) \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \Big]
    \leq C_0^p \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \left\|{X^i_{k} - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{k}}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \bigg].
\end{align*}
Thus, we can rewrite \eqref{HoppingDistEst1} as
\begin{align*}
    & \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \left\|{X^i_{k+1} - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{k+1}}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg]
    \\ \leq \,  &2^{p-1} \bigg(  C_0^p \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \left\|{X^i_{k} - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{k}}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \bigg] + \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_k)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg] \bigg).
\end{align*}
We note that the constant $C_0$ arising from Lemma \ref{CarrL2} depends only on $(\alpha, p, M, C_1, C_2, C_3, R)$, and in particular not on $N$.
Now, define
\begin{align*}
A_k := \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \left\|{X^i_{k} - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{k}}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \bigg].
\end{align*}
Then the previous estimate becomes
\begin{align*}
A_{k+1}
\leq 2^{p-1} C_0^p A_k
+ 2^{p-1}\mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_k)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg].
\end{align*}
Iterating this recursion down to time $0$ yields
\begin{align}
    A_{k+1}
    &\leq \big( 2^{p-1}C_0^p \big)^{k+1} A_0
    + 2^{p-1}\sum_{l=0}^{k}  \big( 2^{p-1}C_0^p \big)^{k-l} \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_l) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_l)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg] \notag\\
    &= 2^{p-1}\sum_{l=0}^{k}  \big( 2^{p-1}C_0^p \big)^{k-l} \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_l) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_l)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg],
    \label{HoppQMF}
\end{align}
where we used $A_0=0$ in the last step, since both systems share the same initial condition. It remains to bound the expectation involving the empirical mean-field measure $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k$ and the mean-field law $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_k$.
For every $k \leq K$, define
\begin{align*}
\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\Omega}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\Omega}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\Omega_{M,k}
:=
\left\{
\omega \in \Omega :
\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^N \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_k}\right\|^p \le M
\right\}.
\end{align*}
Since
\begin{align*}
\Omega_M \subseteq
\left\{
\omega \in \Omega :
\sup_{0 \leq l \leq K} \frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^N \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_l}\right\|^p \le M
\right\}
\subseteq \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\Omega}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\Omega}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\Omega_{M,k},
\end{align*}
we have
\begin{align*}
\mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \le \mathbbm{1}_{\overline{\Omega}_{M,k}}.
\end{align*}
Hence we can use Lemma \ref{CH:ConsBound} in \eqref{HoppQMF} to obtain
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_k)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg]
    \leq
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_k)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\overline{\Omega}_{M,k}} \bigg]
    \leq C_{\mathrm{EM}} N^{-\frac{p}{2}}.
\end{align*}
Inserting this estimate into \eqref{HoppQMF} yields for every $k \leq K$:
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \left\|{X^i_{k+1} - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{k+1}}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \bigg]
    &\leq 2^{p-1}\sum_{l=0}^{k}  \big( 2^{p-1}C_0^p \big)^{k-l}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_l) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_l)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg] \\
    &\leq 2^{p-1} C_{\mathrm{EM}} N^{-\frac{p}{2}}
    \sum_{l=0}^{k}  \big( 2^{p-1}C_0^p \big)^{k-l}.
\end{align*}
Define
\begin{align*}
a_H := 2^{p-1}C_0^p,
\qquad
b_H := 2^{p-1}C_{\mathrm{EM}}.
\end{align*}
Then the previous estimate reads
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \left\|{X^i_{k+1} - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{k+1}}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \bigg]
    \leq b_H N^{-\frac{p}{2}} \sum_{l=0}^{k} a_H^{\,k-l}
    = b_H N^{-\frac{p}{2}} \sum_{l=0}^{k} a_H^{\,l}.
\end{align*}
Since $X_0^i$ and $ \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_0^i$ share the same initial condition, we have
\begin{align*}
\mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{X_0^i-\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_0^i}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \Big]=0,
\end{align*}
and therefore
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K}}{\sup}
    \left\|{ X^i_k - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_k}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg]
    &\leq \sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E} \bigg[ 
    \left\|{ X^i_k - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_k}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg] \\
    &\leq b_H N^{-\frac{p}{2}}
    \sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{l=0}^{k-1} a_H^{\,l} \\
    &= b_H N^{-\frac{p}{2}}
    \sum_{l=0}^{K-1}(K-l)a_H^{\,l}.
\end{align*}
Hence, we take
\begin{align}
    \varepsilon^{\mathrm{rest}}_{\mathrm{H}}(N,K)
    :=
    2^{p-1}C_{\mathrm{EM}} N^{-\frac{p}{2}}
    \Bigg(\sum_{l=0}^{K-1}(K-l)(2^{p-1}C_0^p)^l\Bigg).
    \label{MFH:RestPoCBound}
\end{align}
Finally, if $\mathbb{P}(\Omega_M)>0$, then by the definition of conditional expectation,
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K}}{\sup}
    \left\|{ X^i_k - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_k}\right\|^p \Big| \Omega_M \bigg]
    =
    \frac{\mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K}}{\sup}
    \left\|{ X^i_k - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_k}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg]}{\mathbb{P}(\Omega_M)}
    \leq \frac{\varepsilon^{\mathrm{rest}}_{\mathrm{H}}(N,K)}{\mathbb{P}(\Omega_M)}.
\end{align*}
This concludes the proof of Theorem \ref{MFH: Thrm}.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- remark-9: body_md -->
Recall the propagation-of-chaos estimate for the freezing scheme \eqref{FreezingRestrExplicitEpsilon} with $J=K$ freezing intervals and pass to the limit $s \longrightarrow \infty$ (which in this case means $g \longrightarrow 0$):
   \begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \,\left\|{ X^i_t - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p  \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg] 
    &\leq 2\cdot 2^{p-1}(1-g) C_{\mathrm{EM}}N^{-\frac{p}{2}}
    \sum_{l=0}^{K-1}(K-l)\big( g+ 2^{p-1}(1-g)C_0^p \big)^{l}\\
    &\xrightarrow[]{s \to \infty}
    2\cdot 2^{p-1} C_{\mathrm{EM}}N^{-\frac{p}{2}}
    \sum_{l=0}^{K-1}(K-l)\big( 2^{p-1}C_0^p \big)^{l}.
    \end{align*}
By \eqref{MFH:RestPoCBound}, the corresponding estimate for the hopping scheme is
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K}}{\sup} \left\|{ X^i_k - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_k}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \bigg]
    \leq 2^{p-1} C_{\mathrm{EM}} N^{-\frac{p}{2}}
    \sum_{l=0}^{K-1}(K-l)\big( 2^{p-1}C_0^p \big)^{l}.
\end{align*}
Thus, the two estimates have the same structure, differing only in the appearance of the factor $2$ in the freezing case. This reflects the convergence of the freezing scheme to the hopping scheme as $s \to \infty$, proven in Theorem \ref{thm:FStoHS}.
<!-- end remark-9 -->
<!-- lemma-16: body_md -->
Let $p \geq 1$, let $f$ fulfill \eqref{A1}, \eqref{A2} and \eqref{A3}, and let $\mu, \nu \in \mathcal{P}_p(\mathbb{R}^d)$ be such that there exists $M > 0$ with
    \begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E}_\mu \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big] \leq M.
    \end{align*}
    Then there exists a constant $C = C(\alpha, p, M, C_1, C_2, C_3, R)$ such that
    \begin{align*}
        \left\|{X^\alpha(\mu) - X^\alpha(\nu)}\right\| \leq C W_p(\mu, \nu).
    \end{align*}
\label{GerberStability2}
<!-- end lemma-16 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
Define $\eta_\rho^\alpha := \frac{\omega_\alpha \rho}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\rho})}}$ for $\rho \in \mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^d)$. By Lemma \ref{GerberPropA4}, there exists a constant $K>0$ such that for any $\rho \in \mathcal{P}_p(\mathbb{R}^d)$,
\begin{align}
    \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho)}\right\|
    = \left\|{\mathbb{E}_{\eta^\alpha_\rho}[x]}\right\|
    \leq \mathbb{E}_{\eta^\alpha_\rho}[\left\|{x}\right\|]
    \leq K \mathbb{E}_\rho \big[ \left\|{x}\right\| \big]
    \leq K \mathbb{E}_\rho \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big]^{\frac{1}{p}}
    = K W_p(\rho, \delta_0).
    \label{Corollary33Stability}
\end{align}
We assume that the claim is false. Then for every $i \in \mathbb{N}$ there exist measures $\mu_i, \nu_i \in \mathcal{P}_p(\mathbb{R}^d)$ such that
\begin{align*}
\mathbb{E}_{\mu_i} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big] \leq M
\end{align*}
and
\begin{align}
    \frac{\left\|{X^\alpha(\mu_i) - X^\alpha(\nu_i)}\right\|}{W_p(\mu_i, \nu_i)} \geq i.
    \label{Corollary33Contra}
\end{align}
In particular, this implies that
$
W_p(\mu_i,\nu_i) \xrightarrow[]{i\to\infty} 0
$
cannot hold. Note that if $(\mathbb{E}_{\nu_i} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big])_i$ were bounded, then both $(\mu_i)_i$ and $(\nu_i)_i$ would have uniformly bounded $p$th moments, and Lemma \ref{CarrL2} would yield
\begin{align*}
\left\|{X^\alpha(\mu_i)-X^\alpha(\nu_i)}\right\| \leq C' W_p(\mu_i,\nu_i)
\end{align*}
for some constant $C' >0$ independent of $i$, contradicting \eqref{Corollary33Contra}. Hence
\begin{align*}
\mathbb{E}_{\nu_i} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big] \xrightarrow[]{i \rightarrow \infty} \infty.
\end{align*}
Since
\begin{align*}
W_p^p(\nu_i,\delta_0)=\mathbb{E}_{\nu_i}\big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big],
\end{align*}
it follows that
\begin{align*}
W_p(\nu_i,\delta_0)\xrightarrow[]{i\to\infty}\infty.
\end{align*}
By the inverse triangle inequality, for every $i \in \mathbb{N}$,
\begin{align}
    W_p(\mu_i, \nu_i)
    \geq W_p(\nu_i, \delta_0) - W_p(\mu_i, \delta_0)
    \geq W_p(\nu_i, \delta_0) - M^{1/p}.
    \label{Corollary33Est1}
\end{align}
Moreover, for every $i$,
\begin{align}
    \left\|{X^\alpha(\mu_i) - X^\alpha(\nu_i)}\right\|
    &\leq \left\|{X^\alpha(\mu_i)}\right\| + \left\|{X^\alpha(\nu_i)}\right\| \notag\\
    &\leq K\big(M^{1/p} + W_p(\nu_i, \delta_0)\big),
    \label{Corollary33Est2}
\end{align}
where we used \eqref{Corollary33Stability} in the last inequality. Combining \eqref{Corollary33Est1} and \eqref{Corollary33Est2}, we obtain
\begin{align*}
    \underset{i \rightarrow \infty}{\operatorname*{limsup}}
    \frac{\left\|{X^\alpha(\mu_i) - X^\alpha(\nu_i)}\right\|}{W_p(\mu_i, \nu_i)}
    &\leq
    \underset{i \rightarrow \infty}{\operatorname*{limsup}}
    \frac{\left\|{X^\alpha(\mu_i) - X^\alpha(\nu_i)}\right\|}{W_p(\nu_i, \delta_0) - M^{1/p}} \\
    &\leq
    K \underset{i \rightarrow \infty}{\operatorname*{limsup}}
    \frac{W_p(\nu_i, \delta_0) + M^{1/p}}{W_p(\nu_i, \delta_0) - M^{1/p}}
    = K,
\end{align*}
which contradicts \eqref{Corollary33Contra}. This completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- lemma-17: body_md -->
Let $0< p < q$ and  $\nu \in \mathcal{P}_q(\mathbb{R}^d)$ with $\mathbb{E}_\nu \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^q \big] \leq M_q$. Let $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\nu}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\nu}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\nu^N$ denote the empirical measure of $N$ independent random variables $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_1, ..., \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_N \sim \nu$. Then there exists a constant $C_{\mathrm{EM}}>0$, that only depends on $(f, \alpha, p, q, M_q)$ such that for every $N \in \mathbb{N}$
    \begin{align*}
         \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\nu}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\nu}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\nu^N) - X^\alpha(\nu)}\right\|^p \bigg] \leq C_{\mathrm{EM}} N^{-\frac{p}{2}}.
    \end{align*}
\label{GerberLemma3.7}
<!-- end lemma-17 -->
<!-- theorem-6: body_md -->
Let $T>0$ be the final time. Consider the interacting particle system $\mathcal{X}^N_{T}$ defined by the SDE system (\ref{Delta:Scheme}) and the coupled mean-field system $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\mathcal{X}^N_{T}$ defined by (\ref{Delta:MFScheme}), both sharing the same initial data $\mathcal{X}^N_0 = \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\mathcal{X}^N_0$ almost surely, with common distribution $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_0 \in \mathcal{P}_q(\mathbb{R}^d)$ for some $q \geq 2$. Then, for every $ 1 \leq p \leq q/2$, there exists a $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{D}}^{\mathrm{unrest}}(N, T)>0$ with $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{D}}^{\mathrm{unrest}}(N, T) \xrightarrow[]{N \rightarrow \infty}0$ such that for every $N \in \mathbb{N}$ and every $i \leq N$
    \begin{align*}
          \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \,\left\|{ X^i_t - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \bigg] \leq \varepsilon_{\mathrm{D}}^{\mathrm{unrest}}(N, T).
    \end{align*}
    \label{NPDelta:Thrm}
<!-- end theorem-6 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
For fixed ${\Tilde{T}} \in [0, T], i \leq N$, with the exact same reasoning as in Theorem \ref{Delta:Thrm}, we get:
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E}\bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, \Tilde{T}]}{\sup} \left\|{F^i_t}\right\|^p \bigg] 
    \leq (s\lambda)^p {\Tilde{T}}^{p-1} \int_0^{\Tilde{T}} 
    \mathbb{E}\big[ \left\|{D_\tau}\right\|^p  \big]\, d\tau,
    \label{NPDeltaThrm:Bound1}
\end{align}
where $F^i_t = X^i_t - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t$ and $D_t = X^\alpha(\rho^N_t) -X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_t)$ for every $t \in [0, {\Tilde{T}}]$.   
We now expand $\left\|{D_\tau}\right\|^p$ by inserting the empirical mean-field measure $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_\tau$ and applying the triangle inequality:
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E}\big[ \left\|{D_\tau }\right\|^p \big] 
    &= \mathbb{E}\Big[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_\tau) 
    - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_\tau) }\right\|^p \Big] \notag \\
    &\leq 2^{p-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_\tau) 
    - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_\tau) }\right\|^p \Big] 
    + 2^{p-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_\tau) 
    - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_\tau)}\right\|^p \Big].
    \label{NPDeltaThrm:Bound2}
\end{align}
In order to bound the expectation of the two empirical measures in (\ref{NPDeltaThrm:Bound2}), we introduce 
\begin{align*}
    \Omega_{N, t} := \bigg \{ \omega \in \Omega: \frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i=1} \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p < M \bigg \}
    \quad \text{and} \quad
    Z_i := \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \, \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p,
\end{align*}
where $M> \mathbb{E}\big[ Z_i \big]$ is fixed. The uniform boundedness of expectations of $Z_i$ follows from Lemma \ref{NPDelta:UniformMoment}. This allows us to split as follows: 
\begin{align}
    &\mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_\tau) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_\tau)}\right\|^p \Big] \notag \\
    = \, &\mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_\tau) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_\tau)}\right\|^p  \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_{N, \tau}} \Big]
    + \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_\tau) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_\tau)}\right\|^p  \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega \setminus \Omega_{N, \tau}} \Big].
\end{align}
The estimation of the first part is straightforward, as we can directly use the improved stability estimate of Lemma \ref{GerberStability2} and Example \ref{WasserBD} to get
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_\tau) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_\tau)}\right\|^p \, \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_{N, \tau}} \Big]
    \leq C^p_0 \underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max} \, \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^i_\tau - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_\tau}\right\|^p \bigg]
    = C^p_0 \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^i_\tau - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_\tau}\right\|^p \bigg]
    \notag \\
    \leq C^p_0 \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{s \in [0, \tau]}{\sup}\left\|{X^i_s - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_s}\right\|^p \bigg].
    \label{NPDeltaThrm:Bound3}
\end{align}
To the second summand, we apply Hölder's inequality with conjugate exponents $\frac{q}{p}$ and $\frac{q}{q-p}$:
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_\tau) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_\tau) }\right\|^p\mathbbm{1}_{\Omega \setminus \Omega_{N, \tau}} \Big]
    \leq \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_\tau) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_\tau)}\right\|^q \Big]^{\frac{p}{q}} 
    \, \mathbb{P}(\Omega \setminus \Omega_{N, \tau})^{\frac{q-p}{q}}.
    \label{NPDeltaProbabilitysplit}
\end{align}
We note that almost surely
\begin{align*}
\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^N \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_\tau}\right\|^p \leq \frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^N Z_i,
\end{align*}
and therefore
\begin{align*}
\mathbb{P}(\Omega \setminus \Omega_{N,\tau})
=
\mathbb{P}\bigg(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^N \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_\tau}\right\|^p \ge M\bigg)
\leq
\mathbb{P}\bigg( \frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^N Z_i \ge M \bigg).
\end{align*}
By Lemma \ref{GerberLemma2.5} with $r = q/p$ and the fact that $\mathbb{E}\Big[ Z_1^{q/p} \Big]<\infty$, there exists $K>0$ independent of $N$ such that
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{P}(\Omega \setminus \Omega_{N, \tau})
    \leq K N^{- \frac{q}{2p}}.
\end{align*}
It remains to bound
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_\tau) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_\tau)}\right\|^q \Big]
    \leq 2^{q-1}\bigg( \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_\tau)}\right\|^q \Big] + \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{ X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_\tau)}\right\|^q \Big] \bigg).
    \label{NPDeltaTwoCOnsensus Bound 1}
\end{align}
Using Corollary \ref{GerberPropA4Corollary} with exponent $q$, we get
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_\tau)}\right\|^q \Big]
    \leq b_q \, \mathbb{E} \Big[ \mathbb{E}_{\rho^N_\tau} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^q \big] \Big]
    = b_q \, \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i=1} \left\|{X^i_\tau}\right\|^q \Bigg]
    = b_q \, \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{X^i_\tau}\right\|^q \Big]
    \leq b_q \, C_\mathrm{D},
\end{align*}
and analogously
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_\tau)}\right\|^q \Big]
    \leq b_q \, \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{C}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}C_\mathrm{D},
\end{align*}
where the constants $C_D$ and $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{C}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}C_D$ arise from Lemma \ref{NPDelta:UniformMoment} with $q$ as the order of moment.
Thus,
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_\tau) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_\tau)}\right\|^q \Big]
    \leq 2^{q-1} b_q (C_\mathrm{D} + \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{C}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}C_\mathrm{D}).
\end{align*}
Hence \eqref{NPDeltaProbabilitysplit} yields
\begin{align}
     \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_\tau) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_\tau) }\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega \setminus \Omega_{N, \tau}} \Big]
     \leq \Big(2^{q-1} b_q (C_\mathrm{D} + \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{C}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}C_\mathrm{D})\Big)^{\frac{p}{q}} K^{\frac{q-p}{q}} N^{-\frac{q-p}{2p}}
     =: C N^{-\frac{q-p}{2p}},
     \label{NPDeltaThrm:Bound4}
\end{align}
for a constant $C>0$ independent of $N$.
Combining (\ref{NPDeltaThrm:Bound3}) and (\ref{NPDeltaThrm:Bound4}) yields
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_\tau) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_\tau)}\right\|^p \Big]
    \leq C^p_0 \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{s \in [0, \tau]}{\sup}\left\|{X^i_s - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_s}\right\|^p \bigg]
    + C N^{-\frac{q-p}{2p}}.
    \label{NPDeltaThrm:T1Bound}
\end{align}
For the second term of \eqref{NPDeltaThrm:Bound2}, we use Lemma \ref{GerberLemma3.7}:
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \left\|{X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_\tau) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_\tau)}\right\|^p \bigg]
    \leq C_{\mathrm{EM}} N^{-\frac{p}{2}}.
    \label{NPDelta BoundEM}
\end{align}
Hence
\begin{align*}
  \mathbb{E}\big[ \left\|{D_\tau }\right\|^p \big]
  \leq 2^{p-1} \Bigg( 
  C^p_0 \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \sup_{s \in [0, \tau]}\left\|{X^i_s - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_s}\right\|^p \bigg]
  + C N^{-\frac{q-p}{2p}}
  + C_{\mathrm{EM}} N^{-\frac{p}{2}}
   \Bigg).
\end{align*}
Define
\begin{align*}
r_* := \min \bigg \{ \frac{p}{2}, \frac{q-p}{2p} \bigg \}.
\end{align*}
Then
\begin{align*}
  \mathbb{E}\big[ \left\|{D_\tau }\right\|^p \big]
  \leq 2^{p-1} \Bigg( 
  C^p_0 \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \sup_{s \in [0, \tau]}\left\|{X^i_s - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_s}\right\|^p \bigg]
  + (C+C_{\mathrm{EM}}) N^{-r_*}
   \Bigg).
\end{align*}
Putting this into \eqref{NPDeltaThrm:Bound1}:
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E}\bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, \Tilde{T}]}{\sup} \left\|{F^i_t}\right\|^p \bigg] 
    &\leq (s\lambda)^p {\Tilde{T}}^{p-1} \int_0^{\Tilde{T}}
    \mathbb{E}\big[ \left\|{D_\tau}\right\|^p  \big]\, d\tau \\
    &\leq (2\Tilde{T})^{p-1}(s\lambda  C_0)^p \int_0^{\Tilde{T}} 
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \sup_{s \in [0, \tau]}\left\|{X^i_s - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_s}\right\|^p \bigg]\, d\tau \\
    & \quad + 2^{p-1} (s\lambda {\Tilde{T}})^p  (C + C_{\mathrm{EM}}) N^{-r_*}.
\end{align*}
Applying Grönwall’s lemma (Lemma \ref{GronwallLemma}) yields
\begin{align*}
     \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \,\left\|{ X^i_t - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \bigg]
     \leq 2^{p-1} (s\lambda T)^p  (C + C_{\mathrm{EM}})
     e^{2^{p-1}(s\lambda T C_0)^p}
     N^{-r_*}.
\end{align*}
Defining
\begin{align*}
\varepsilon^{\mathrm{unrest}}_{\mathrm{D}}(N,T)
:=
2^{p-1} (s\lambda T)^p (C + C_{\mathrm{EM}}) e^{2^{p-1}(s\lambda T C_0)^p}
N^{- r_* },
\end{align*}
concludes the proof.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- remark-10: body_md -->
If $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_0$ has bounded moments of order $q \geq p^2 +p$, this constitutes for every $p \geq 1$ propagation of chaos at rate $\mathcal{O}\big( N^{-1/2} \big)$. This is the rate stated in \cite[Theorem 2.6]{Gerber2025Mean-fieldSampling} for classical CBO under the assumption of bounded moments of all orders.
<!-- end remark-10 -->
<!-- theorem-7: body_md -->
Let $T>0$ be the final time. Consider the interacting particle system $\mathcal{X}^N_{T}$ defined by the SDE system (\ref{CF:CFscheme}) and the coupled mean-field system $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\mathcal{X}^N_{T}$ defined by (\ref{CF:MFIPS}), both sharing the same initial data $\mathcal{X}^N_0 = \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\mathcal{X}^N_0$ almost surely, with common distribution $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_0 \in \mathcal{P}_q(\mathbb{R}^d)$ for some $q \geq 2$. Then, for every $1 \leq p \leq q/2$, there exists a $\varepsilon^{\mathrm{unrest}}_{\mathrm{F}}(N, T) >0$ with $\varepsilon^{\mathrm{unrest}}_{\mathrm{F}}(N, T) \xrightarrow[]{N \rightarrow \infty}0$ such that for every $N \in \mathbb{N}$ and every $i \leq N$
    \begin{align*}
          \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \,\left\|{ X^i_t - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \bigg] \leq \varepsilon^{\mathrm{unrest}}_{\mathrm{F}}(N, T).
    \end{align*}
    \label{NPFreezing:Thrm}
<!-- end theorem-7 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
We let $j \in \{ 0, ..., J-1 \}$ be an arbitrary index and fix an $i \leq N$. With the same reasoning as in Theorem \ref{MFF: Thrm}, for every $j \in \{0,\dots,J-1\}$ we have
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{t \in [t_j, t_{j+1})}{\sup} \left\|{X^i_{t} - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{t} }\right\|^p \bigg]
    &=
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \max \Big \{ \left\|{E^{(i)}_j}\right\|^p, \left\|{E^{(i)}_{j+1}}\right\|^p \Big \} \bigg] \notag\\
    &\leq
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{E^{(i)}_j}\right\|^p + \left\|{E^{(i)}_{j+1}}\right\|^p  \bigg].
    \label{NP:CFIntervalSupBound}
\end{align}
Hence it is enough to estimate
\begin{align*}
A_j := \mathbb{E}\bigg[ \left\|{E^{(i)}_j}\right\|^p \bigg], \qquad j=0,\dots,J.
\end{align*}
Since $E^{(i)}_0=0$ almost surely, we have $A_0=0$.

Using \eqref{MFF:DistanceScheme} alongside with the convexity of $x \mapsto \left\|{x}\right\|^p$, we estimate
\begin{align}
     A_{j+1}
     &= \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{gE^{(i)}_{j} + D_{j}(1-g)}\right\|^p \bigg] \notag\\
    &\leq 
    g A_j + (1-g)\mathbb{E}\big[ \left\|{D_{j}}\right\|^p \big].
    \label{NP:CFBound1}
\end{align}
To bound the distance between the two consensus points
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E}\big[ \left\|{D_{j}}\right\|^p \big] = \mathbb{E}\bigg[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_j}) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_{t_j})}\right\|^p \bigg],
\end{align*}
we use once again the same triangle inequality argument by employing the empirical measure of the mean-field particle system:
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E}\bigg[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_j}) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_{t_j})}\right\|^p \bigg]
    \leq 2^{p-1} \Bigg( \mathbb{E}\bigg[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_j}) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_{t_j})}\right\|^p \bigg] + \mathbb{E}\bigg[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_{t_j}) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_{t_j})}\right\|^p \bigg] \Bigg).
    \label{NP:CFConsDecomp1}
\end{align}
For the distance between the consensus points of the two empirical measures, we use the decomposition described in the introduction of this chapter:
\begin{align}
    &\mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_j}) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_{t_j})}\right\|^p \Big] \notag \\
    =\, &\mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_j}) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_{t_j})}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_{N, {t_j}}} \Big] + \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_j}) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_{t_j})}\right\|^p  \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega \setminus \Omega_{N, {t_j}}} \Big],
    \label{NP:CFConsSplit2}
\end{align}
where
\begin{align*}
    \Omega_{N, t} := \bigg \{ \omega \in \Omega: \frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i=1} \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p < M \bigg \}.
\end{align*}
On $\Omega_{N,t_j}$, the empirical mean-field measure $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_{t_j}$ satisfies $\mathbb{E}_{\overline{\rho}^N_{t_j}}\big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big] \le M$, 
so Lemma \ref{GerberStability2} applies and yields
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_j}) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_{t_j})}\right\|^p \, \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_{N, {t_j}}} \Big]
    &\leq C^p_0  \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i=1}\left\|{X^i_{t_j} - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{t_j}}\right\|^p \bigg] \notag \\ 
    &= C^p_0  \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^i_{t_j} - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{t_j}}\right\|^p \bigg] \notag \\
    &= C^p_0 A_j.
    \label{NP:CFConsBound1}
\end{align}
On $\Omega \setminus \Omega_{N, t_j}$, we make use of Hölder's inequality with conjugate exponents $\frac{q}{p}$ and $\frac{q}{q-p}$ to separate the indicator function: 
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_j}) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_{t_j}) }\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega \setminus \Omega_{N, {t_j}}} \Big] 
    &\leq \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_j}) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_{t_j})}\right\|^q \Big]^{\frac{p}{q}} \, \mathbb{P}(\Omega \setminus \Omega_{N, {t_j}})^{\frac{q-p}{q}}.
    \label{NPFreezingProbability split}
\end{align}
We define the random variables $Z_1, ..., Z_N$ by $Z_i := \underset{0 \leq j \leq J}{\sup} \, \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{t_j}}\right\|^p$ for every $i \leq N$.
Note that $\mathbb{E}\Big[ \big |Z_1 \big |^{q/p} \Big] < \infty$ by the regularity of the solution of (\ref{CF:MFIPS}) introduced by Lemma \ref{NP:CFLemma}. Therefore, by Lemma \ref{GerberLemma2.5} with $r = q/p$, for $M> 0$ such that $\mathbb{E}\big[  |Z_1 | \big] < M$, there exists a constant $K > 0$ independent of $N$ such that for every $N \in \mathbb{N}$
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{P}(\Omega \setminus \Omega_{N, {t_j}})
    = \mathbb{P}\bigg( \frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i=1} \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{t_j}}\right\|^p \geq M \bigg)
    \leq \mathbb{P} \bigg( \frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i=1} Z_i \geq M \bigg)
    \leq K N^{- \frac{q}{2p}}.
\end{align*}
This means that we only have to control $\mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_j}) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_{t_j})}\right\|^q \Big]$ by a constant independent of $N$. Therefore, we can use the very straightforward bound
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_j}) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_{t_j})}\right\|^q \Big]
    \leq 2^{q-1}\bigg( \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_j})}\right\|^q \Big] + \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{ X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_{t_j})}\right\|^q \Big] \bigg).
    \label{NPFRTwoCOnsensus Bound 1}
\end{align}
We can use Corollary \ref{GerberPropA4Corollary} with exponent $q$ to control
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_j})}\right\|^q \Big]
    \leq b_q \, \mathbb{E} \Big[ \mathbb{E}_{\rho^N_{t_j}} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^q \big] \Big]
    = b_q \, \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i=1} \left\|{X^i_{t_j}}\right\|^q \Bigg]
    = b_q \, \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{X^i_{t_j}}\right\|^q \Big]
    \leq b_q \, C_\mathrm{F},
\end{align*}
where the last inequality follows from Lemma \ref{NP:CFLemma}. Analogously, we get
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_{t_j})}\right\|^q \Big] \leq b_q \,  \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{C}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}C_\mathrm{F}.
\end{align*}
We stress that both bounds are independent of $N$. 
We move on to combine the latter estimates with \eqref{NPFRTwoCOnsensus Bound 1}:
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_j}) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_{t_j})}\right\|^q \Big]
    \leq 2^{q-1} b_q (C_\mathrm{F} + \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{C}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}C_\mathrm{F}).
\end{align*}
Thus, we can write estimate \eqref{NPFreezingProbability split} as follows:
 \begin{align}
     \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_j}) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_{t_j}) }\right\|^p\mathbbm{1}_{\Omega \setminus \Omega_{N, {t_j}}} \Big]
     &\leq \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_j}) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_{t_j})}\right\|^q \Big]^{\frac{p}{q}} \, \mathbb{P}(\Omega \setminus \Omega_{N, {t_j}})^{\frac{q-p}{q}} \notag \\
     &\leq \Big(2^{q-1} b_q (C_F + \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{C}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}C_F)\Big)^{\frac{p}{q}} K^{\frac{q-p}{q}} N^{-\frac{q-p}{2p}}
     =: C N^{-\frac{q-p}{2p}}.
     \label{NP:CFConsBound2}
 \end{align}
Putting \eqref{NP:CFConsBound1} and \eqref{NP:CFConsBound2} into \eqref{NP:CFConsSplit2} yields
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_j}) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_{t_j})}\right\|^p \Big]
    \leq C^p_0 A_j + C N^{-\frac{q-p}{2p}}.
    \label{NP:CFConsBound3}
\end{align}
We now move on to making an effort to obtain a useful estimate of the distance between the consensus of the empirical mean-field measure $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_{t_j}$ and the actual mean-field measure $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_{t_j}$.
We can directly apply Lemma \ref{GerberLemma3.7} to obtain
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \left\|{X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_{t_j}) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_{{t_j}})}\right\|^p \bigg] \leq C_{\mathrm{EM}} N^{-\frac{p}{2}}.
\end{align*}
As in the proof of Theorem \ref{MFF: Thrm}, we point out that the above bound is independent of $j$.
Finally, we can combine the latter with \eqref{NP:CFConsBound3} to get for \eqref{NP:CFConsDecomp1}
\begin{align*}
     \mathbb{E}\bigg[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_j}) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_{t_j})}\right\|^p \bigg]
     &\leq 2^{p-1} \Bigg( \mathbb{E}\bigg[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_{t_j}) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_{t_j})}\right\|^p \bigg] + \mathbb{E}\bigg[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_{t_j}) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_{t_j})}\right\|^p \bigg] \Bigg)\\
     &\leq 2^{p-1} \Bigg( C^p_0 A_j + C N^{-\frac{q-p}{2p}} + C_{\mathrm{EM}} N^{-\frac{p}{2}} \Bigg)\\
     &\leq 2^{p-1} \Bigg( C^p_0 A_j + (C+C_{\mathrm{EM}}) N^{- \min \big \{ \frac{p}{2}, \frac{q-p}{2p} \big \} } \Bigg).
\end{align*}
We insert the above in \eqref{NP:CFBound1} and obtain
\begin{align}
    A_{j+1}
    &\leq g A_j + (1-g) 2^{p-1} \Bigg( C^p_0 A_j + (C + C_{\mathrm{EM}}) N^{- \min \big \{ \frac{p}{2}, \frac{q-p}{2p} \big \} } \Bigg) \notag \\
    &= \Big( g + (1-g)2^{p-1}C^p_0 \Big)A_j + 2^{p-1} (1-g) (C+ C_{\mathrm{EM}}) N^{- \min \big \{ \frac{p}{2}, \frac{q-p}{2p} \big \} }.
    \label{NP:CFRecursion}
\end{align}
Define
\begin{align*}
    a_F &:= g + 2^{p-1}(1-g) C_0^p,\\
    b_F &:= 2^{p-1}(1-g) (C + C_{\mathrm{EM}}),\\
    r_* &:= \min \bigg \{ \frac{p}{2}, \frac{q-p}{2p} \bigg \}.
\end{align*}
Then \eqref{NP:CFRecursion} becomes
\begin{align*}
A_{j+1} \leq a_F A_j + b_F N^{-r_*}.
\end{align*}
Since $A_0=0$, we obtain by induction
\begin{align}
    A_{j+1}
    \leq b_F N^{-r_*} \sum^j_{l = 0} a_F^l.
    \label{NP:CF:Thrm:ItEstimate}
\end{align}
We note that by \eqref{NP:CFIntervalSupBound},
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \,\left\|{ X^i_t - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \bigg]
    &= \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \underset{ 0 \leq {j} \leq {J-1}}{\sup} \,  \underset{t \in [t_j, t_{j+1})}{\sup} \,\left\|{ X^i_t - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \bigg] \notag\\
    &\leq \sum_{j=0}^{J-1} \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{t \in [t_j, t_{j+1})}{\sup} \,\left\|{ X^i_t - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \bigg] \notag\\
    &\leq \sum_{j=0}^{J-1} \big(A_j + A_{j+1}\big) \notag\\
    &\leq 2\sum_{j=1}^{J} A_j.
\end{align*}
Finally, combining the latter together with \eqref{NP:CF:Thrm:ItEstimate} gives us
\begin{align}
    & \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \,\left\|{ X^i_t - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \bigg] \notag \\
    \leq \,  &2\sum_{j=1}^{J} A_j \notag\\
    \leq \,  &2 b_F N^{-r_*} \sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum^ {j-1}_{l = 0} a_F^l \notag\\
    = \,  &2 b_F N^{-r_*} \sum_{l = 0}^{J-1} (J-l)a_F^l \notag\\
    = \,  &2 \cdot 2^{p-1}(1-g) (C + C_{\mathrm{EM}}) N^{-r_*} \sum_{l = 0}^{J-1} (J-l)(g + 2^{p-1}(1-g) C_0^p)^l \notag\\
    =: \,  &\varepsilon^{\mathrm{unrest}}_\mathrm{F}(N, T).
    \label{NPFreezing:PoCBound}
\end{align}
Since $i \leq N$ was chosen to be arbitrary, the proof is complete.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- theorem-8: body_md -->
Let $K \in \mathbb{N}_{>0}$ be the final time. Consider the interacting particle system $\mathcal{X}^N_{K}$ defined by the hopping scheme \eqref{CH:Scheme} and the coupled mean-field system $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\mathcal{X}^N_{K}$ defined by \eqref{CH:MFScheme}, both sharing the same initial data $\mathcal{X}^N_0 = \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\mathcal{X}}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\mathcal{X}^N_0$ almost surely, with common distribution $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_0 \in \mathcal{P}_q(\mathbb{R}^d)$ for some $q \geq 2$. Then for every $1 \leq p \leq q/2$, there exists a $\varepsilon_\mathrm{H}^{\mathrm{unrest}}(N, K)>0$ with $\varepsilon_\mathrm{H}^{\mathrm{unrest}}(N, K) \xrightarrow[]{N \rightarrow \infty}0$ such that for every $N \in \mathbb{N}$ and every $i \leq N$
    \begin{align*}
          \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K}}{\sup} \,\left\|{ X^i_k - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_k}\right\|^p \bigg] \leq \varepsilon_\mathrm{H}^{\mathrm{unrest}}(N, K).
    \end{align*}
    \label{NPHopping:Thrm}
<!-- end theorem-8 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
We fix $i \leq N$ and $k \leq K$ and use the definition of \eqref{CH:Scheme} and \eqref{CH:MFScheme} together with a basic inequality to obtain
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{ X^i_{k+1} - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{k+1}}\right\|^p \bigg]
    &= \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_k) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_k)}\right\|^p \bigg]\notag \\
    &\leq 2^{p-1} \bigg( \underbrace{\mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_k) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k)}\right\|^p \bigg]}_{=: S_1} + \underbrace{\mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_k)}\right\|^p \bigg]}_{=: S_2} \bigg).
\end{align}
$S_2$ can be estimated by exploiting the relation between the mean-field empirical measure $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k$ and $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_k$. 
The estimation of $S_1$ is the crucial part that requires the decomposition described in the introduction of this chapter. We will use the same line of argument as in Theorems \ref{NPDelta:Thrm} and \ref{NPFreezing:Thrm}. We start by introducing
\begin{align*}
    \Omega_{N, k} := \bigg \{ \omega \in \Omega: \frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i=1} \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_k}\right\|^p < M \bigg \}
\end{align*}
in order to split 
\begin{align}
    &\mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_k) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k)}\right\|^p \bigg] \notag \\
    = \, &\mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_k) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_{N, k}} \bigg] + \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_k) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega \setminus \Omega_{N, k}} \bigg]. 
\end{align}
In the first summand, we can directly apply Lemma \ref{GerberStability2} and Example \ref{WasserBD} together with the exchangeability of the particles to get the estimate
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_k) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_{N, k}} \bigg]
    \leq C^p_0 \mathbb{E} \Big[ W^p_p (\rho^N_k, \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k) \Big]
    &\leq C^p_0 \underset{1 \leq i \leq N}{\max}\mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{X^i_k - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_k}\right\|^p \Big] \notag \\
    &\leq  C^p_0 \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{X^i_k - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_k}\right\|^p \Big].
    \label{NP:CH:BoundComplement}
\end{align}
To the second summand, we apply Hölder's inequality with conjugate exponents $\frac{q}{p}$ and $\frac{q}{q-p}$:
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_k) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k)}\right\|^p\mathbbm{1}_{\Omega \setminus \Omega_{N, k}} \Big]
    \leq \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_k) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k)}\right\|^q \Big]^{\frac{p}{q}} \, \mathbb{P}(\Omega \setminus \Omega_{N, k})^{\frac{q-p}{q}}.
    \label{NPHoppingProbability split}
\end{align}
For the probability part, we can make use of Lemma \ref{GerberLemma2.5}. We note that by defining $Z_i := \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K}}{\sup} \, \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_k}\right\|^p$, it immediately follows that $\mathbb{E}\Big[ \big |Z_1 \big |^{q/p} \Big] < \infty$ by the regularity of (\ref{CH:MFScheme}) justified by Lemma \ref{NP_CHLemma}. Therefore, by Lemma \ref{GerberLemma2.5} with $r = q/p$, for $M> 0$ such that $\mathbb{E}\big[  |Z_1 | \big] < M$, there exists a constant $K_0 > 0$ independent of $N$ such that for every $N \in \mathbb{N}$
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{P}(\Omega \setminus \Omega_{N, k})
    = \mathbb{P}\bigg( \frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i=1} \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_k}\right\|^p \geq M \bigg)
    \leq \mathbb{P} \bigg( \frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i=1} Z_i \geq M \bigg)
    \leq K_0 N^{- \frac{q}{2p}}.
\end{align*}
It remains to control $\mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_k) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k)}\right\|^q \Big]$ by a constant independent of $N$. As in the preceding proofs of this chapter, we can use the very straightforward bound
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_k) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k)}\right\|^q \Big]
    \leq 2^{q-1}\bigg( \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_k)}\right\|^q \Big] + \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{ X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k)}\right\|^q \Big] \bigg).
    \label{NPCHTwoCOnsensus Bound 1}
\end{align}
Corollary \ref{GerberPropA4Corollary} with exponent $q$ allows us to control
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_k)}\right\|^q \Big]
    \leq b_q \, \mathbb{E} \Big[ \mathbb{E}_{\rho^N_k} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^q \big] \Big]
    = b_q \, \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i=1} \left\|{X^i_k}\right\|^q \Bigg]
    = b_q \, \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{X^i_k}\right\|^q \Big]
    \leq b_q \, C_\mathrm{H},
\end{align*}
where the last inequality follows from Lemma \ref{NP_CHLemma}. Analogously, we get
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k)}\right\|^q \Big] \leq b_q \,  \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{C}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}C_\mathrm{H}.
\end{align*}
We stress that both bounds are independent of $N$. 
We move on to combine the latter estimates with \eqref{NPCHTwoCOnsensus Bound 1}:
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_k) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k)}\right\|^q \Big]
    \leq 2^{q-1} b_q (C_\mathrm{H} + \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{C}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}C_\mathrm{H}).
\end{align*}
Thus, we can write estimate \eqref{NPHoppingProbability split} as follows:
 \begin{align}
     \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_k) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k) }\right\|^p\mathbbm{1}_{\Omega \setminus \Omega_{N, k}} \Big]
     &\leq \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_k) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k)}\right\|^q \Big]^{\frac{p}{q}} \, \mathbb{P}(\Omega \setminus \Omega_{N, k})^{\frac{q-p}{q}} \notag \\
     &\leq \Big(2^{q-1} b_q (C_\mathrm{H} + \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{C}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{C}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}C_\mathrm{H})\Big)^{\frac{p}{q}} K_0^{\frac{q-p}{q}} N^{-\frac{q-p}{2p}}
     =: C N^{-\frac{q-p}{2p}}.
     \label{NP:CH:BoundOnSet}
 \end{align}
 The combination of \eqref{NP:CH:BoundComplement} and \eqref{NP:CH:BoundOnSet} yields
 \begin{align*}
    S_1 =  \mathbb{E} \Big[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\rho^N_k) - X^\alpha (\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k)}\right\|^p \Big]
    \leq C^p_0 \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{X^i_k - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_k}\right\|^p \Big] + C N^{-\frac{q-p}{2p}}.
 \end{align*}
For $S_2$, we can directly use Lemma \ref{GerberLemma3.7} to obtain a constant $C_{\mathrm{EM}}>0$ independent of $N$ such that
\begin{align*}
    S_2 = \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_k) - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_k)}\right\|^p \bigg] \leq C_{\mathrm{EM}} N^{-\frac{p}{2}}.
\end{align*}
Define
\begin{align*}
r_* := \min \bigg\{ \frac{p}{2}, \frac{q-p}{2p} \bigg\},
\qquad
a_H := 2^{p-1}C_0^p,
\qquad
b_H := 2^{p-1}(C+C_{\mathrm{EM}}).
\end{align*}
Then we obtain
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{ X^i_{k+1} - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{k+1}}\right\|^p \bigg]
    &\leq 2^{p-1} \Big( S_1 + S_2 \Big) \\
    &\leq 2^{p-1} \Bigg( C^p_0 \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{X^i_k - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_k}\right\|^p \Big] + C N^{-\frac{q-p}{2p}} + C_{\mathrm{EM}} N^{-\frac{p}{2}} \Bigg) \\
    &\leq a_H \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{X^i_k - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_k}\right\|^p \Big]
    + b_H N^{-r_*}.
\end{align*}
Since $X_0^i=\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_0^i$ almost surely, iterating this recursion yields
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{ X^i_{k+1} - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_{k+1}}\right\|^p \bigg]
    \leq b_H N^{-r_*}\sum^{k}_{l = 0} a_H^l.
    \label{NP:CH:Thrm:FinalEstimate}
\end{align}
Hence
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K}}{\sup} \,\left\|{ X^i_k - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_k}\right\|^p \bigg]
    &\leq \sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{ X^i_k - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_k}\right\|^p \bigg] \\
    &\leq b_H N^{-r_*}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum^{k-1}_{l = 0} a_H^l \\
    &= b_H N^{-r_*}\sum_{l=0}^{K-1}(K-l)a_H^l \\
    &= 2^{p-1}(C+C_{\mathrm{EM}})N^{-r_*}
    \sum_{l=0}^{K-1}(K-l)(2^{p-1}C_0^p)^l
    =: \varepsilon_\mathrm{H}^{\mathrm{unrest}}(N,K).
\end{align*}
This concludes the proof.
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- remark-11: body_md -->
We want to compare our mean-field estimate for hopping with the one obtained in the freezing case \eqref{NPFreezing:PoCBound}. Recall that for the freezing scheme we proved
    \begin{align*}
        &\mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \,\left\|{ X^i_t - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \bigg]\\
        \leq \, 
        &2 \cdot 2^{p-1}(1-g) (C + C_{\mathrm{EM}}) N^{-r_*}
        \sum_{l = 0}^{J-1} (J-l)\big( g + 2^{p-1}(1-g) C_0^p \big)^l.
    \end{align*}
    If we now take $J=K$ and pass to the limit $s \longrightarrow \infty$ (which means $g \longrightarrow 0$), we obtain
    \begin{align*}
        \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{t \in [0, T]}{\sup} \,\left\|{ X^i_t - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_t}\right\|^p \bigg]
        \xrightarrow[]{s \to \infty}
        2 \cdot 2^{p-1}(C + C_{\mathrm{EM}}) N^{-r_*}
        \sum_{l = 0}^{K-1} (K-l)\big( 2^{p-1} C_0^p \big)^l.
    \end{align*}
    By the hopping estimate, we have
    \begin{align*}
        \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{ 0 \leq {k} \leq {K}}{\sup} \,\left\|{ X^i_k - \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i_k}\right\|^p \bigg]
        \leq
        2^{p-1}(C + C_{\mathrm{EM}}) N^{-r_*}
        \sum_{l = 0}^{K-1} (K-l)\big( 2^{p-1} C_0^p \big)^l.
    \end{align*}
    Thus, the two estimates have almost the same bound, differing only by the appearance of the factor $2$ in the freezing case. This reflects the convergence of the freezing scheme to the hopping scheme as $s \to \infty$, proven in Theorem \ref{thm:FStoHS}.
<!-- end remark-11 -->
<!-- remark-12: body_md -->
We observe that in the case where the initial distribution $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_0$ has finite moments of order $q \geq p^2+p$, Theorems \ref{NPDelta:Thrm}, \ref{NPFreezing:Thrm}, and \ref{NPHopping:Thrm} yield propagation of chaos in Wasserstein-$p$ distance at rate $\mathcal{O}(N^{-1/2})$, which is consistent with the results in \cite{Gerber2025Mean-fieldSampling, Fornasier2024Consensus-BasedGlobally}. If $2p \leq q < p^2 +p$, the rate of convergence reduces to $\mathcal{O}(N^{-(q-p)/2p^2})$, which deteriorates as $p$ increases. Note that $q \geq 2p$ implies $\frac{q-p}{2p^2} \geq \frac{1}{2p}$, so the unrestricted rate is never worse than $\mathcal{O}(N^{-1/(2p)})$.
<!-- end remark-12 -->
<!-- theorem-9: body_md -->
\label{thm:invariant}
    For any $r>0$ and 
    $\alpha$ sufficiently large depending on $r$,
    the map $\mathcal{T}^\alpha$ defined by \cref{eq:T_alpha} restricted to $B_r(x^*)$ is a contraction mapping with Lipschitz constant $L_\alpha$, where $L_\alpha\to 0$ as $\alpha\to\infty$.
    Furthermore, for $\alpha$ sufficiently large, the dynamics \eqref{CF:MFscheme} has a unique invariant measure $\boldsymbol\rho_\infty^\alpha$ with the property that $\boldsymbol\mu_\infty^\alpha \coloneqq \mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\rho}_\infty^\alpha}[x] \in B_r(x^*)$, and $\boldsymbol{\rho}_\infty^\alpha$ is of the form
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:invariant}
        \boldsymbol\rho_\infty^\alpha=\mathcal{N}\left(\boldsymbol\mu_\infty^\alpha,\frac{\delta^2}{2\lambda}I_d\right)=\mathcal{N}\left(\mathcal{T}^\alpha(\boldsymbol\mu_\infty^\alpha),\frac{\delta^2}{2\lambda}I_d\right)=\mathcal{N}\left(X^\alpha(\boldsymbol\rho_\infty^\alpha),\frac{\delta^2}{2\lambda}I_d\right).
    \end{equation}
Furthermore it holds that
 \begin{equation}
     W_2(\boldsymbol\rho_\infty^\alpha, \boldsymbol{\rho}_\infty)\to 0\quad \mbox{ as }\alpha\to\infty \,,
 \end{equation}
 with $\boldsymbol{\rho}_\infty= \mathcal{N}\left(x^*,\frac{\delta^2}{2\lambda}I_d\right)$\,.
<!-- end theorem-9 -->
<!-- theorem-10: body_md -->
\label{thm:global_bound}
   Let $r \in \mathbb{N}_{\geq 2}$ and let $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_0 \in \mathcal{P}_r(\mathbb{R}^d)$ be an arbitrary initial measure. Let $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_t$ be the solution to the consensus freezing scheme \eqref{CF:MFscheme}. Then, there exist $\alpha_0 > 0$ and $S > 0$ such that for all $\alpha \geq \alpha_0$, the solution satisfies
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:second_moment_bound}
        \mathbb{E}_{\overline{\rho}_t} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^q \big] \leq S, \quad \forall t \geq 0, \quad \forall q \in \mathbb{N} \text{ with } q \leq r,
    \end{equation}
    and
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:gamma_t_alpha_bound}
        \left\|{X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_t)}\right\| \leq S, \quad \forall t \geq 0.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-10 -->
<!-- theorem-11: body_md -->
\label{thm:fsconv}
    Let $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_{t}$ be the unique solution to the dynamics \eqref{CF:MFscheme} with the initial data $X_0\sim \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_0 {\in \mathcal{P}_4(\mathbb{R}^d)}$ (not necessarily a Gaussian) and any $\Delta t>0$. {Let $r=2S$}, {where $S$ is a constant fulfilling \cref{eq:second_moment_bound,eq:gamma_t_alpha_bound} such that $x^* \in B_S(0)$ and $\mathbb{E}_{\rho_0}[x] \in B_S(0)$}, and assume  $\boldsymbol\rho_\infty^\alpha=\mathcal{N}(\boldsymbol\mu_\infty^\alpha,\frac{\delta^2}{2\lambda}I_d)$ is the invariant measure found in Theorem \ref{thm:invariant} {with $\boldsymbol\mu_\infty^\alpha\in B_r(x^*)$}. Then, for {any $  e^{-s \lambda \Delta t}<\gamma<1$} and for $\alpha>0$ sufficiently large it holds that
    \begin{equation}
       W_2(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_{t},\boldsymbol\rho_\infty^\alpha)\leq C_\alpha \gamma^j\ \quad \mbox{ for } t \in [t_j, t_{j+1}), \ j \in \mathbb{N}_{0} \,,
    \end{equation}
    for some $C_\alpha>0$. Furthermore, for any $s^\star > 0$, we may choose $C_\alpha$ and $\gamma$ uniformly over all $s\in [s^\star, +\infty)$.
<!-- end theorem-11 -->
<!-- theorem-12: body_md -->
\label{thm:global_conv_mean_T}
Let $T > 0$, $\Delta t > 0$, $s > 0$, $\lambda > 0$, and let $J = T/\Delta t \in \mathbb{N}$ be the number of freezing steps. Let $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_0 \in \mathcal{P}_6(\mathbb{R}^d)$ be the common initial distribution, not necessarily Gaussian. Let $(\mathcal{X}^N_t)_t$ be the interacting particle system defined by \eqref{CF:CFscheme}, and define
\begin{align*}
M_T^N := \frac1N \sum_{i=1}^N X_T^i.
\end{align*}
Let $\boldsymbol\rho_\infty^\alpha=\mathcal{N}(\boldsymbol\mu_\infty^\alpha,\frac{\delta^2}{2\lambda}I_d)$ be the unique invariant measure from Theorem \ref{thm:invariant}. For any $\gamma \in (e^{-s\lambda\Delta t},1)$ and $\alpha$ sufficiently large, there exists a constant $C_\alpha>0$ and a constant $C(T)>0$, independent of $N$, such that for every $\varepsilon>0$ and every $N\in\mathbb N$,
\begin{align}
    \mathbb P\bigg(
    \left\|{M_T^N-\boldsymbol\mu^\alpha_\infty}\right\|^2
    \ge 3C_\alpha^2\gamma^{2J}+\varepsilon
    \bigg)
    \le \frac{C(T)}{\varepsilon N}.
    \label{eq:global_mean_T}
\end{align}
<!-- end theorem-12 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
Let $(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^1, ..., \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^N_T)$ be the coupled mean-field particle system \eqref{CF:MFIPS} at time $T$ and define
\begin{align*}
\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{M}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{M}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}M_T^N := \frac1N \sum_{i=1}^N \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^i.
\end{align*}
We decompose
\begin{align*}
M_T^N-\boldsymbol\mu^\alpha_\infty
=
\big(M_T^N-\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{M}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{M}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}M_T^N\big)
+
\big(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{M}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{M}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}M_T^N-\mathbb E[\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^1]\big)
+
\big(\mathbb E[\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^1]-\boldsymbol\mu^\alpha_\infty\big).
\end{align*}
Hence, by the inequality $\left\|{a+b+c}\right\|^2 \le 3(\left\|{a}\right\|^2+\left\|{b}\right\|^2+\left\|{c}\right\|^2)$ and taking expectations,
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{M_T^N-\boldsymbol\mu^\alpha_\infty}\right\|^2 \Big]
    \le
    3 \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{M_T^N-\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{M}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{M}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}M_T^N}\right\|^2 \Big]
    +3\mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{M}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{M}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}M_T^N-\mathbb E[\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^1]}\right\|^2 \Big]
    +3\left\|{\mathbb E[\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^1]-\boldsymbol\mu^\alpha_\infty}\right\|^2.
\end{align*}
We begin with the deterministic term.
