Since $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_T=\mathrm{Law}(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^1)$ and $\boldsymbol\rho_\infty^\alpha=\mathcal N\big( \boldsymbol\mu_\infty^\alpha,\frac{\delta^2}{2\lambda}I_d \big)$, Jensen's inequality yields
\begin{align*}
    \left\|{\mathbb E[\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^1]-\boldsymbol\mu^\alpha_\infty}\right\|^2
    =
    \left\|{\int x\,d\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_T(x)-\int y\,d \boldsymbol \rho_\infty^\alpha(y)}\right\|^2
    &\le \inf_{\pi\in\Pi(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_T, \boldsymbol\rho_\infty^\alpha)}
\int \left\|{x-y}\right\|^2\,d\pi(x,y) \\ &=
    W_2^2(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_T, \boldsymbol\rho_\infty^\alpha).
\end{align*}
By Theorem \ref{thm:fsconv}, for $\alpha$ sufficiently large and $\gamma\in(e^{-s\lambda\Delta t},1)$,
\begin{align*}
W_2(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_T,\boldsymbol \rho_\infty^\alpha)\le C_\alpha\gamma^J,
\end{align*}
and therefore
\begin{align*}
\left\|{\mathbb E[\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^1]-\boldsymbol\mu^\alpha_\infty}\right\|^2
\le C_\alpha^2\gamma^{2J}.
\end{align*}
It remains to control the two random terms.
For the first term, by the convexity of $x \mapsto \left\|{x}\right\|^2$, Jensen's inequality gives almost surely
\begin{align*}
    \left\|{M_T^N-\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{M}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{M}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}M_T^N}\right\|^2
    &=
    \left\|{
    \frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N (X_T^i-\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^i)
    }\right\|^2
    \le
    \frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N \left\|{X_T^i-\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^i}\right\|^2.
\end{align*}
Taking expectations and using Theorem \ref{NPFreezing:Thrm} with $p=2$ and $q=6$, we obtain
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb E \bigg[ \left\|{M_T^N-\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{M}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{M}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}M_T^N}\right\|^2 \bigg]
    &\le
    \frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N \mathbb E\bigg[ \left\|{X_T^i-\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^i}\right\|^2 \bigg]
    \le
    \varepsilon_{\mathrm{F}}^{\mathrm{unrest}}(N,T)
    =: \frac{C_{\mathrm{PoC}}(T)}{N}.
\end{align*}
Hence, by Markov's inequality,
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb P\bigg(
    3\left\|{M_T^N-\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{M}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{M}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}M_T^N}\right\|^2 \ge \frac{\varepsilon}{2}
    \bigg)
    \le
    \frac{6\,C_{\mathrm{PoC}}(T)}{\varepsilon N}.
\end{align*}
For the second term, since $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^1,\dots,\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^N$ are i.i.d. with mean $\mathbb E[\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^1]$, we have

\begin{align*}
    \mathbb E\bigg[ \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{M}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{M}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}M_T^N-\mathbb E[\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^1]}\right\|^2 \bigg]
    &=
    \mathbb E\Bigg[ \left\|{\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N \big(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^i-\mathbb E[\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^1]\big)}\right\|^2 \Bigg] \\
    &=
    \frac1{N^2}\sum_{i=1}^N
    \mathbb E\bigg[ \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^i-\mathbb E[\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^1]}\right\|^2 \bigg] \\
    &=
    \frac1N\,\mathbb E\bigg[ \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^1-\mathbb E[\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^1]}\right\|^2 \bigg] \\
    &=
    \frac1N\Bigg( \mathbb E\bigg[ \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^1}\right\|^2 \bigg]
    - \left\|{\mathbb E[\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^1]}\right\|^2 \Bigg) \\
    &\le
    \frac1N\,\mathbb E\bigg[ \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^1}\right\|^2 \bigg]
    =
    \frac1N\,\mathbb E_{\overline{\rho}_T}\bigg[ \left\|{x}\right\|^2 \bigg].
\end{align*}
By Theorem \ref{thm:global_bound}, the second moment of $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_T$ is bounded uniformly in time, and therefore
\begin{align*}
\mathbb E\bigg[ \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{M}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{M}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}M_T^N-\mathbb E[\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^1]}\right\|^2 \bigg]
\le \frac{S}{N}.
\end{align*}
Again by Markov's inequality,
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb P\bigg(
    3\left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{M}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{M}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}M_T^N-\mathbb E[\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^1]}\right\|^2 \ge \frac{\varepsilon}{2}
    \bigg)
    \le
    \frac{6S}{\varepsilon N}.
\end{align*}
Combining the previous estimates, we get
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb P\bigg(
    \left\|{M_T^N-\boldsymbol\mu^\alpha_\infty}\right\|^2
    \ge 3C_\alpha^2\gamma^{2J}+\varepsilon
    \bigg)
    &\le
    \mathbb P\bigg(
    3\left\|{M_T^N-\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{M}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{M}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}M_T^N}\right\|^2
    +
    3\left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{M}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{M}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}M_T^N-\mathbb E[\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^1]}\right\|^2
    \ge \varepsilon
    \bigg) \\
    &\le
    \mathbb P\bigg(
    3\left\|{M_T^N-\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{M}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{M}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}M_T^N}\right\|^2 \ge \frac{\varepsilon}{2}
    \bigg)
    +
    \mathbb P\bigg(
    3\left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{M}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{M}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}M_T^N-\mathbb E[\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_T^1]}\right\|^2 \ge \frac{\varepsilon}{2}
    \bigg) \\
    &\le
    \frac{6(C_{\mathrm{PoC}}(T)+S)}{\varepsilon N}.
\end{align*}
Thus the claim follows with
\begin{align}
C(T):=6\big(C_{\mathrm{PoC}}(T)+S\big).\label{eq:global_IPS_TT}
\end{align}
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- remark-13: body_md -->
The estimate in Theorem \ref{thm:global_conv_mean_T} is meaningful for every fixed time horizon $T>0$, but it is not uniform in time. The bottleneck is the constant
\begin{align*}
C(T)=6\big(C_{\mathrm{PoC}}(T)+S\big),
\end{align*}
and more precisely the propagation of chaos contribution $C_{\mathrm{PoC}}(T)$. The propagation of chaos constant $C_{\mathrm{PoC}}(T)$ grows exponentially in $T$, since it is inherited from the finite-horizon propagation of chaos estimates established earlier in this thesis. Hence, although the deterministic convergence term $C_\alpha^2\gamma^{2J}$ decays exponentially fast as $T\to\infty$, the prefactor in the probabilistic bound deteriorates at the same time.
In order to guarantee
\begin{align*}
\mathbb P\bigg(
    \left\|{M_T^N-\boldsymbol\mu^\alpha_\infty}\right\|^2
    \le 3C_\alpha^2\gamma^{2J}+\varepsilon
\bigg)\ge 1-\xi,
\end{align*}
it is sufficient to choose
\begin{align*}
N \ge \frac{C(T)}{\varepsilon \xi}.
\end{align*}
Thus, the number of particles required to maintain a confidence level of at least $1-\xi$ grows proportionally to $C(T)$. Since $C_{\mathrm{PoC}}(T)$ grows exponentially in $T$, the same is true for the required particle number. Therefore, Theorem \ref{thm:global_conv_mean_T} does not yield a global-in-time convergence result for the interacting particle system with a fixed number of particles. If $C(T)$ were bounded uniformly in $T$, then one could choose $N=\mathcal O((\varepsilon\xi)^{-1})$ independently of the time horizon and obtain a genuine uniform-in-time high-probability estimate. Thus, the main obstruction to a full global convergence result for the interacting particle system is the lack of a propagation of chaos estimate that remains uniform in time.
<!-- end remark-13 -->
<!-- remark-14: body_md -->
We might ask ourselves whether, instead of proving convergence at the level of the empirical mean, one could aim directly for a bound on
\begin{align*}
\mathbb{E} \Big[W^2_2\big(\rho_T^N,\boldsymbol\rho_\infty^\alpha\big)\Big].
\end{align*}
The triangle inequality yields
\begin{align*}
\mathbb{E} \Big[W^2_2\big(\rho_T^N,\boldsymbol\rho_\infty^\alpha\big)\Big]
\leq
2\mathbb{E} \Big[W^2_2\big(\rho_T^N,\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_T\big)\Big]
+
2W^2_2\big(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_T,\boldsymbol\rho_\infty^\alpha\big),
\end{align*}
where $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_T$ denotes the law of the mean-field dynamics at time $T$. To estimate the first term, one may further decompose
\begin{align*}
\mathbb{E}\Big[W^2_2\big(\rho_T^N,\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_T\big)\Big]
\leq
2\mathbb{E}\Big[W^2_2\big(\rho_T^N,\overline{\rho}_T^N\big)\Big]
+
2\mathbb{E}\Big[W^2_2\big(\overline{\rho}_T^N,\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_T\big)\Big],
\end{align*}
where
\begin{align*}
\overline{\rho}_T^N:=\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\delta_{\overline X_T^i}
\end{align*}
is the empirical measure of the coupled i.i.d. mean-field particles.

The first term can be controlled by Example \ref{WasserBD} and Theorem \ref{NPFreezing:Thrm} to get a similar estimate to that of Theorem \ref{thm:global_conv_mean_T} at rate $\mathcal{O}(N^{-1/2})$. To bound the second term, we need to use Lemma \ref{Pre:ConvEmp}. In particular, for $p=2, q = 6$,
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \big[ W^2_2(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_T, \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_T) \big] \leq C M_q^{1/3} \begin{cases}
             N^{-1/2} + N^{- 2/3} \quad &\text{if } d<4 \\
             N^{-1/2}\log (1+ N) + N^{- 2/3} \quad &\text{if } d=4, \\
             N^{-2/d} + N^{- 2/3} \quad &\text{if } d>4 \text{ and } \frac{d}{d-2} \neq 6,
         \end{cases}
\end{align*}
where $C> 0$ is a constant depending on the dimension $d$.
Hence, while we still recover the rate $\mathcal{O}(N^{-1/2})$ in low dimension, the estimate deteriorates as soon as $d>4$, where the leading term becomes $\mathcal{O}(N^{-2/d})$. Hence, the convergence rate deteriorates as the dimension $d$ increases.
For this reason, the global convergence results in this section are formulated at the level of the empirical mean rather than directly in Wasserstein distance for the empirical measure.
<!-- end remark-14 -->
<!-- theorem-13: body_md -->
\label{thm:FStoHS}
    Let $x_0 \in \mathbb{R}^d$ be an arbitrary initial point. Let $\{x_k^{CH}\}_{k \in \mathbb{N}}$ be the solution to the Consensus Hopping scheme \eqref{HoppingSchemeOriginal} with initial data $x_0$ and $\tilde{\sigma} = \frac{\delta}{\sqrt{2\lambda}}$. Furthermore, for $s > 0$, let $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_t$ be the solution to the Consensus Freezing scheme \eqref{CF:MFscheme} with initial data $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_0 = \mathcal{N}(x_0, \Sigma)$, where $\Sigma = \frac{\delta^2}{2\lambda} I_d$, and using the prescribed $s$. The evolution of $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_t$ is given by
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:flip_scheme_solution_s}
    \begin{split}
        \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_t &= \mathcal{N}(\mu^{\alpha}_t, \Sigma), \qquad \text{where} \\
        \mu^{\alpha}_t &= \mathcal{T}^\alpha(\mu^{\alpha}_{t_i})+e^{-s \cdot \lambda(t-t_i)}(\mu^{\alpha}_{t_i}-\mathcal{T}^\alpha(\mu^{\alpha}_{t_i})), \quad t \in (t_i, t_{i+1}].
    \end{split}
\end{equation}
    Then, for large enough $\alpha$, we have that
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:FS_to_HS}
        |\mu^{\alpha}_{t_k} - x_k^{CH}| \leq 4 R \cdot e^{-s \lambda \Delta t} \to 0 \quad \text{as } s \to \infty, \quad \forall k \in \mathbb{N},
    \end{equation}
    where $R > 0$ is a uniform bound on $\mu_t^s$ given by global-in-time bounds of the freezing scheme such that $x^* \in B_R(0)$.
    In particular, we have that
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:W2_FS_to_HS}
        W_2(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_{t_k}, \mathcal{N}(x_k^{CH}, \Sigma)) \leq 4 R \cdot e^{-s \lambda \Delta t} \to 0 \quad \text{as } s \to \infty,
    \end{equation}
    {uniformly with respect to $k\in \mathbb N$}.
<!-- end theorem-13 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{cor:HS_convergence}
 Let $x_0 \in \mathbb{R}^d$ be an arbitrary initial point. Let \newline $\{x_k^{CH}\}_{k \in \mathbb{N}}$ be the solution to the Consensus Hopping scheme \eqref{HoppingSchemeOriginal} with initial data $x_0$ and $\tilde{\sigma} = \frac{\delta}{\sqrt{2\lambda}}$. Moreover, for any $k\in\mathbb{N}$, let $\nu_k^\alpha=\mathcal N(x_k^{CH}, \frac{\delta^2}{2\lambda}I)$. 
 Then, for any $\gamma \in (0, 1)$ and for $\alpha$ sufficiently large, it holds that
 \begin{equation*}
        W_2(\nu_k^\alpha, \boldsymbol\rho_\infty^\alpha) \leq C_\alpha \gamma^k,
 \end{equation*}
 for some $C_\alpha > 0$ depending on $\alpha$.
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- theorem-14: body_md -->
\label{cor:global_conv_hopping}
Let $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_0 \in \mathcal{P}_6(\mathbb{R}^d)$ be the common initial distribution, not necessarily Gaussian, and let the noise in \eqref{CH:Scheme} have variance $\sigma^2 = \frac{\delta^2}{2\lambda}$. Let $(\mathcal{X}^N_k)_k$ be the interacting particle system defined by \eqref{CH:Scheme}, and define
\begin{align*}
M_K^N := \frac1N \sum_{i=1}^N X_K^i.
\end{align*}
Let $\boldsymbol\rho_\infty^\alpha=\mathcal{N}(\boldsymbol\mu_\infty^\alpha,\frac{\delta^2}{2\lambda}I_d)$ be the unique invariant measure from Theorem \ref{thm:invariant}. For any $\gamma \in (0,1)$ and $\alpha$ sufficiently large, there exist constants $C_\alpha>0$ and $C(K)>0$, independent of $N$, such that for every $\varepsilon>0$, every $K \in \mathbb{N}_{>0}$ and every $N\in\mathbb N$,
\begin{align}
    \mathbb P\bigg(
    \left\|{M_K^N-\boldsymbol\mu^\alpha_\infty}\right\|^2
    \ge 3C_\alpha^2\gamma^{2(K-1)}+\varepsilon
    \bigg)
    \le \frac{C(K)}{\varepsilon N}.
    \label{eq:global_mean_K}
\end{align}
<!-- end theorem-14 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
The argument is the same as in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:global_conv_mean_T}; the propagation of chaos term is now controlled by Theorem~\ref{NPHopping:Thrm} instead of Theorem~\ref{NPFreezing:Thrm}, and the deterministic mean-field convergence term by Corollary~\ref{cor:HS_convergence} instead of Theorem~\ref{thm:fsconv}.
Let
\begin{align*}
\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{M}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{M}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}M_K^N := \frac1N \sum_{i=1}^N \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_K^i
\end{align*}
denote the empirical mean of the coupled mean-field particle system. We decompose
\begin{align*}
M_K^N-\boldsymbol\mu^\alpha_\infty
=
\big(M_K^N-\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{M}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{M}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}M_K^N\big)
+
\big(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{M}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{M}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}M_K^N-\mathbb E[\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_K^1]\big)
+
\big(\mathbb E[\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_K^1]-\boldsymbol\mu^\alpha_\infty\big).
\end{align*}
For the first term, Theorem \ref{NPHopping:Thrm} with $p=2$ and $q=6$ yields, exactly as in the proof of Theorem \ref{thm:global_conv_mean_T},
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb E \bigg[ \left\|{M_K^N-\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{M}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{M}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}M_K^N}\right\|^2 \bigg]
    \le
    \frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N \mathbb E\bigg[ \left\|{X_K^i-\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_K^i}\right\|^2 \bigg]
    \le
    \varepsilon_{\mathrm{H}}^{\mathrm{unrest}}(N,K)
    =: \frac{C_{\mathrm{PoC}}(K)}{N}.
\end{align*}
For the second term, we use that $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_K^1,\dots,\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_K^N$ are i.i.d.\ with law $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_K = \mathcal{N}\big( X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_{K-1}), \sigma^2 I_d \big)$, so that
\begin{align*}
\mathbb E\bigg[ \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{M}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{M}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}M_K^N-\mathbb E[\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_K^1]}\right\|^2 \bigg]
= \frac{1}{N}\, \mathbb E\bigg[ \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_K^1-\mathbb E[\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_K^1]}\right\|^2 \bigg]
= \frac{d\sigma^2}{N}.
\end{align*}
It remains to treat the deterministic term. By \eqref{CH:MFScheme}, for every $k \geq 1$ we have $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_k = \mathcal{N}(m_k, \sigma^2 I_d)$ with $m_k := X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_{k-1})$. In particular, $m_1 = X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_0)$, and since $\sigma^2 = \frac{\delta^2}{2\lambda}$, the definition \eqref{eq:T_alpha} of $\mathcal{T}^\alpha$ gives
\begin{align*}
    m_k = X^\alpha\big( \mathcal{N}(m_{k-1}, \sigma^2 I_d) \big) = \mathcal{T}^\alpha(m_{k-1}), \quad k \geq 2.
\end{align*}
The consensus hopping scheme \eqref{HoppingSchemeOriginal} satisfies $x^{CH}_k = \mathcal{T}^\alpha(x^{CH}_{k-1})$ as well. Hence, choosing the initial point $x_0 := X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_0)$ in Corollary \ref{cor:HS_convergence}, we obtain $m_k = x^{CH}_{k-1}$ and therefore $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_k = \nu^\alpha_{k-1}$ for every $k \geq 1$. Jensen's inequality and Corollary \ref{cor:HS_convergence} yield
\begin{align*}
\left\|{\mathbb E[\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_K^1]-\boldsymbol\mu^\alpha_\infty}\right\|^2
\le
W_2^2(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_K,\boldsymbol\rho_\infty^\alpha)
=
W_2^2(\nu^\alpha_{K-1},\boldsymbol\rho_\infty^\alpha)
\le
C_\alpha^2\gamma^{2(K-1)}.
\end{align*}
Proceeding with Markov's inequality exactly as in the proof of Theorem \ref{thm:global_conv_mean_T}, we obtain \eqref{eq:global_mean_K} with
\begin{align*}
    C(K) := 6\big( C_{\mathrm{PoC}}(K) + d\sigma^2 \big).
\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- lemma-18: body_md -->
Let $(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X
    }\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X
    }\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X
    _t)_{t \geq 0 }$ be the $\delta$-CBO mean-field process \eqref{Delta:MFSDE} with initial law $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_0 \in \mathcal{P}_2(\mathbb{R}^d)$. Then, for every $t \geq 0$,
    \begin{align}
        \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_t - \mathbb{E}\Big[ \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_t \Big]}\right\|^2  \bigg] = e^{-2s\lambda t} \, \mathbb{E} \bigg[  \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_0 - \mathbb{E}\Big[ \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_0 \Big]}\right\|^2  \bigg] + \frac{d \delta^2}{2\lambda} \big( 1 - e^{-2 s \lambda t} \big) \xrightarrow[t \to \infty]{} \frac{d\delta^2}{2\lambda} \, > 0 .
        \label{Outlook:OUIdentity}
    \end{align}
    The same identity holds for the consensus freezing scheme \eqref{CF:MFscheme}, and for the consensus hopping scheme one has $\mathbb{E} \bigg[  \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_k - \mathbb{E}\Big[ \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_k \Big]}\right\|^2  \bigg] = d\sigma^2$ for every $k \geq 1$.
    \label{Outlook:LemmaMoment}
<!-- end lemma-18 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
Write $m_t := \mathbb{E}\big[ \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_t \big]$. Since $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_t$ is deterministic, so is $t \mapsto X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_t)$, and it is locally bounded by Corollary \ref{GerberPropA4Corollary}. Taking expectations in \eqref{Delta:MFSDE} therefore yields $\dot m_t = -s\lambda\big( m_t - X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_t) \big)$, and subtracting this from \eqref{Delta:MFSDE} shows that $Y_t := \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_t - m_t$ satisfies
    \begin{align*}
        dY_t = - s \lambda \, Y_t \, dt + \sqrt{s}\delta \, dB_t .
    \end{align*}
    The consensus point cancels exactly, because it enters $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X_t$ and $m_t$ in the same way. Hence $Y$ is an Ornstein--Uhlenbeck process, and
    \begin{align*}
        Y_t = e^{-s\lambda t} Y_0 + \sqrt{s} \delta \int^t_0 e^{-s\lambda(t-\tau)} dB_\tau .
    \end{align*}
    Since $Y_0$ is independent of $(B_t)_{t \geq 0}$ and centered, and the stochastic integral is a centered martingale, the cross term vanishes, and the It\^o isometry gives
   \begin{align}
       \mathbb{E}\big[ \left\|{Y_t}\right\|^2 \big] = e^{-2s\lambda t}\mathbb{E}\big[ \left\|{Y_0}\right\|^2 \big] + s\delta^2 d \int^t_0 e^{-2s\lambda u} du,
   \end{align}
which is \eqref{Outlook:OUIdentity}. For the freezing scheme the frozen consensus point is likewise state-independent, so the same cancellation occurs on each interval and the resulting process is continuous across the $t_j$. For the hopping scheme the claim is immediate, since $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_k = \mathcal{N}\big( X^\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho_{k-1}), \sigma^2 I_d \big)$ for every $k \geq 1$ by \eqref{CH:MFScheme}.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- assumption-1: body_md -->
There exist $c \in (0, 1]$ and a class $\mathcal{C} \subseteq \mathcal{P}_p(\mathbb{R}^d)$ containing the empirical measures $\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{\rho}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{\rho}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}\rho^N_t$ for all $t \geq 0$ and all $N \in \mathbb{N}$, such that
    \begin{align*}
        \left\|{X^\alpha(\nu_z) - X^\alpha(\nu)}\right\| \leq (1 - c) \left\|{z}\right\| \qquad \text{for all } \nu \in \mathcal{C}, \ z \in \mathbb{R}^d .
        \label{Outlook:HypH}
    \end{align*}
    \label{Outlook:AssumptionH}
<!-- end assumption-1 -->
<!-- lemma-19: body_md -->
Let $X \sim \mathcal{N}(0, 1)$. Then for every $t>0$, we have
    \begin{align*}
        \mathbb{P}(X \geq t) \leq \frac{1}{t} \cdot \frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}} e^{- \frac{t^2}{2}}. 
    \end{align*}
\label{Pre:SimpleGaussBound}
<!-- end lemma-19 -->
<!-- proposition-2: body_md -->
Let $N\geq 2$ and $X_1, ...,X_N$ be independent, identically distributed Gaussian random variables such that $X_1\sim \mathcal{N}(0, 1)$. Then there exists a constant $C_p >0$ that depends only on $p$ such that
    \begin{align*}
        \mathbb{E}\bigg[ \underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max} \, |X_i|^p \bigg] \leq C_p (\log N)^{\frac{p}{2}}.
    \end{align*}
    \label{Pre:LemmaNormaVariables}
<!-- end proposition-2 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
We can compute straightforwardly:
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E}\bigg[ \underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max} \, |X_i|^p \bigg] &= \int^\infty_0 \mathbb{P} 
    (\underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max} \, |X_i|^p \geq x) \, dx \\
    (x = t^p) \quad &= \int^\infty_0 \mathbb{P} (\underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max} \, |X_i| \geq t) 
    \, pt^{p-1} \, dt.
\end{align*}
We now choose to split the integral at $t_0 = \sqrt{2 \log N}$:
\begin{align}
    \int^\infty_0  \mathbb{P} (\underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max} \, |X_i| \geq t)  pt^{p-1} \, dt 
    &\leq \int_0^{t_0}  pt^{p-1} \, dt + \int_{t_0}^\infty  
    \mathbb{P} (\underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max} \, |X_i| \geq t)  pt^{p-1} \, dt, 
    \label{Pre:iidBound2}
\end{align}
where we used $\mathbb{P} (\underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max} \, |X_i| \geq t) \leq 1$. 
We evaluate both integrals separately, starting with the first one:
\begin{align}
    \int_0^{t_0}  pt^{p-1} \, dt = t_0^{p} 
    \overset{(t_0 = \sqrt{2 \log N})}{=} \big( 2 \log N \big)^{\frac{p}{2}}.
    \label{Pre:iidFirstIntegralB1}
\end{align}
The analysis of the second integral is somewhat more difficult. 
We begin by applying a union bound in conjunction with the i.i.d. assumption, 
which yields
\begin{align}    
     \int^\infty_{t_0} \mathbb{P} (\underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max} \, |X_i| \geq t)  
     pt^{p-1} \, dt \leq N \int^\infty_{t_0} 
     \mathbb{P} ( |X_1| \geq t) \, pt^{p-1} \, dt.
     \label{Pre:iidSeconintegralNBound}
\end{align}
We make use of Lemma \ref{Pre:SimpleGaussBound} to obtain 
\begin{align}
      N\int^\infty_{t_0} \mathbb{P} ( |X_1| \geq t) \, pt^{p-1} \, dt
   &\leq \frac{2N}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int^\infty_{t_0} \frac{1}{t} \cdot 
   e^{- \frac{t^2}{2}} \cdot pt^{p-1} \, dt \notag \\
   &= \frac{2Np}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int^\infty_{t_0} 
   e^{- \frac{t^2}{2}} t^{p-2} \, dt.
   \label{Pre:iidSeconintegralB1}
\end{align}
We further estimate the integral by inserting $e^{\frac{t_0^2}{2}}
e^{-\frac{t_0^2}{2}}$:
\begin{align}
    \int^\infty_{t_0} e^{- \frac{t^2}{2}} t^{p-2} \, dt 
    &= \int^\infty_{t_0} t^{p-2}  e^{- \frac{t^2 - t_0^2}{2}} 
    e^{-\frac{t^2_0}{2}}\, dt \notag\\
   \Big( \frac{t_0^2}{2} = \log N \Big) \quad 
   &= \frac{1}{N}\int^\infty_{t_0} t^{p-2}  
   e^{- \frac{t^2 - t_0^2}{2}}\, dt \notag\\
\Big( t^2-t_0^2 \geq 2t_0(t-t_0) \Big) \quad   
   &\leq \frac{1}{N}\int^\infty_{t_0} t^{p-2}  
   e^{- t_0(t-t_0)} \, dt \notag\\
\Big( u = t - t_0 \Big) \quad   
   &= \frac{1}{N} \int^\infty_{0} (u + t_0)^{p-2}  
   e^{- t_0u} \, du \notag\\
\Big( (u+t_0)^{p-2} \leq 2^{p-2} (u^{p-2} + t_0^{p-2}) \Big) \quad 
   &\leq \frac{2^{p-2}}{N}\Bigg(  
   \underbrace{\int^\infty_{0} u^{p-2}  e^{-t_0u} \, du}
   _{ = \frac{\Gamma(p-1)}{t_0^{p-1}}} 
   + t_0^{p-2} \underbrace{\int^\infty_{0} e^{-t_0u} \, du}
   _{= \frac{1}{t_0}} \Bigg) \notag \\
&= \frac{2^{p-2}}{N} \bigg( \frac{\Gamma(p-1)}{t_0^{p-1}} 
   + t_0^{p-3} \bigg) \notag\\
\Big( t_0 = \sqrt{2 \log N} \Big) \quad &= \frac{2^{p-2}}{N} \Bigg( \frac{\Gamma(p-1)}{(2 \log N)^{\frac{p-1}{2}}} 
   + (2 \log N)^{\frac{p-3}{2}} \Bigg).
\label{Pre:iidSeconintegralB2}
\end{align}
We now justify that \eqref{Pre:iidSeconintegralB2} is bounded by $K_p \frac{(2\log N)^{\frac{p-3}{2}}}{N}$
for a constant $K_p$ depending only on $p$. Dividing \eqref{Pre:iidSeconintegralB2} by $\frac{(2\log N)^{\frac{p-3}{2}}}{N}$, this reduces to
\begin{align*}
     2^{p-2}\left(\frac{\Gamma(p-1)}{(2\log N)^{p-2}}
    + 1\right) \leq K_p .
\end{align*}
For $N \geq 2$ and $p \geq 2$, we have $(2\log N)^{p-2} \geq (2\log 2)^{p-2}$,
so we can choose explicitly
\begin{align*}
    K_p := 2^{p-2}\bigg( \frac{\Gamma(p-1)}{(2\log 2)^{p-2}}+1 \bigg),
\end{align*}
which is a positive constant depending only on $p$.
\newline
Inserting \eqref{Pre:iidSeconintegralB2} into \eqref{Pre:iidSeconintegralB1} 
gives us
\begin{align*}
    N\int^\infty_{t_0} \mathbb{P} ( |X_1| \geq t) \, pt^{p-1} \, dt
   \leq \frac{2Np}{\sqrt{2\pi}} \cdot \frac{K_p}{N} 
   \big( 2 \log N \big)^{\frac{p-3}{2}} 
   = \frac{2pK_p}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\big( 2 \log N \big)^{\frac{p-3}{2}}.
\end{align*}
This once again combined with \eqref{Pre:iidFirstIntegralB1} yields 
our final estimate
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E}\bigg[ \underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max} \, |X_i|^p \bigg] 
    &\leq \big( 2 \log N \big)^{\frac{p}{2}} 
    + \frac{2pK_p}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\big( 2 \log N \big)^{\frac{p-3}{2}} 
    \leq C_p (\log N)^{\frac{p}{2}}, 
\end{align*}
for a constant $C_p > 0$ depending only on $p$. 
This concludes the proof.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
For simplicity, we first assume that $X_1, ..., X_N$ are i.i.d. standard normal distributed random vectors. Therefore, we can compute straightforwardly
    \begin{align}
       \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max} \left\|{X_i}\right\|^p \bigg] = \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max} \bigg( \sum ^d_{l=1} |X_{i, l}|^2  \bigg)^{\frac{p}{2}} \Bigg],
        \label{Pre:Gaussian ineq.}
    \end{align}
where we note that for every $i\leq N$ and $l \leq d$, $X_{i, l} \sim N(0, 1)$. We use standard inequalities and the i.i.d. property to compute further:
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max} \bigg( \sum ^d_{l=1} |X_{i, l}|^2  \bigg)^{\frac{p}{2}} \Bigg] \leq d^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max} \,   \underset{1 \leq {l} \leq {d}}{\max} \,  |X_{i, l}|^p \Bigg] &\leq d^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \underset{1 \leq {m} \leq {2Nd}}{\max} \, |\widetilde{X}_m|^p \Bigg] \notag \\ &\leq C_p \Big( d\log (2Nd) \Big)^{\frac{p}{2}},
\end{align}
where $(\widetilde{X}_m)_{m \leq 2Nd}$ denotes the family $(X_{i,l})_{i \leq N, l \leq d}$ extended by $Nd$ further independent standard normal random variables. Adding random variables can only increase the maximum, and since $2Nd \geq 2$, we may apply Proposition \ref{Pre:LemmaNormaVariables}.
From this we can directly deduce the result for $Z_1, ..., Z_N \sim N(0, \sigma^2 I_d)$:
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \underset{1 \leq {i} \leq {N}}{\max} \left\|{Z_i}\right\|^p \bigg] \leq \sigma^pC_p \Big( d\log (2Nd) \Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}.
\end{align}
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- theorem-15: body_md -->
Let $p \in [2, \infty)$. Then, there exists a constant $C_{p, d}>0$, depending only on $p$ and $d$ such that for any $N\in \mathbb{N}$, for any collection of independent, zero mean random variables $X_1, ..., X_N$ with finite $p$th moments, we have
    \begin{align*}
        \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \left\|{\sum^N_{i=1} X_i}\right\|^p \Bigg]^{\frac{1}{p}} \leq C_{p, d} \max \Bigg\{ \bigg( \sum^N_{i=1} \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{X_i}\right\|^p \Big] \bigg)^{\frac{1}{p}}, \bigg( \sum^N_{i=1} \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{X_i}\right\|^2 \Big] \bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}  \Bigg \}.
    \end{align*}
    \label{Rosenthal}
<!-- end theorem-15 -->
<!-- corollary-3: body_md -->
Let $p \in [2, \infty)$. Then, there exists a constant $C_{p, d} > 0$, depending only on $p$ and $d$ such that for any $N \in \mathbb{N}$, for any collection of independent, identically distributed, zero mean random variables $X_1, ..., X_N$ with finite $p$th moments, we have
    \begin{align*}
        \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \left\|{\frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i= 1} X_i}\right\|^p \Bigg] \leq C_{p, d} \max \Bigg\{ \frac{\mathbb{E}\big[ \left\|{X_1}\right\|^p \big]}{N^{p-1}},  \frac{\mathbb{E}\big[ \left\|{X_1}\right\|^2 \big]^{\frac{p}{2}}}{N^{\frac{p}{2}}}  \Bigg \}.
    \end{align*}
    \label{RosenthalCorr}
<!-- end corollary-3 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
By Theorem \ref{Rosenthal}, we immediately get
    \begin{align*}
          \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \left\|{ \frac{1}{N}\sum^N_{i=1} X_i}\right\|^p \Bigg]^{\frac{1}{p}} =  \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \left\|{\sum^N_{i=1} \frac{X_i}{N}}\right\|^p \Bigg]^{\frac{1}{p}} &\leq  K_{p, d} \max \Bigg\{ \Bigg( \sum^N_{i=1} \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{\frac{X_i}{N}}\right\|^p \bigg] \Bigg)^{\frac{1}{p}}, \Bigg( \sum^N_{i=1} \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{\frac{X_i}{N}}\right\|^2 \bigg] \Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}  \Bigg \} \\
         \text{(identically distributed)} & =  K_{p, d} \max \Bigg\{ \Bigg( N \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{\frac{X_1}{N}}\right\|^p \bigg] \Bigg)^{\frac{1}{p}}, \Bigg( N \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{\frac{X_1}{N}}\right\|^2 \bigg] \Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}  \Bigg \}\\
         &= K_{p, d} \max \Bigg\{ \Bigg(  \frac{\mathbb{E}\big[ \left\|{X_1}\right\|^p \big]}{N^{p-1}} \Bigg)^{\frac{1}{p}}, \Bigg(  \frac{\mathbb{E}\big[ \left\|{X_1}\right\|^2 \big]}{N} \Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}  \Bigg \}, 
    \end{align*}
where $K_{p, d}>0$ is the constant arising from Theorem \ref{Rosenthal}. Lastly, raising both sides to the power of $p$ and defining $C_{p, d} := K_{p, d}^p$ gives us
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \left\|{ \frac{1}{N}\sum^N_{i=1} X_i}\right\|^p \Bigg] \leq C_{p, d} \max \Bigg\{ \frac{\mathbb{E}\big[ \left\|{X_1}\right\|^p \big]}{N^{p-1}},  \frac{\mathbb{E}\big[ \left\|{X_1}\right\|^2 \big]^{\frac{p}{2}}}{N^{\frac{p}{2}}}  \Bigg \}.
\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
We fix $N \in \mathbb{N}$ and begin to estimate
\begin{align}
     &\mathbb{E} \Bigg[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\overline{\nu}^N) - X^\alpha (\nu)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \Bigg]\\ = \, & \mathbb{E} \Bigg[  \left\|{ \frac{1}{N}\sum^N_{i=1} \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i\frac{\omega_\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i)}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\overline{\nu}^N})}}  -  \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} x \frac{\omega_\alpha(x)}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\nu})}} d\nu(x)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \Bigg] \notag \\
     \leq \, & 2^{p-1}\mathbb{E} \Bigg[  \left\|{ \frac{1}{N}\sum^N_{i=1} \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i\frac{\omega_\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i)}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\overline{\nu}^N})}}  -  \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} x \frac{\omega_\alpha(x)}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\overline{\nu}^N})}} d\nu(x)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \Bigg] \notag \\
     \quad \, & + 2^{p-1}\mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \left\|{ \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} x \frac{\omega_\alpha(x)}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\overline{\nu}^N})}} d\nu(x) -\int_{\mathbb{R}^d} x \frac{\omega_\alpha(x)}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\nu})}} d\nu(x)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \Bigg] \notag \\
     = \, & \colon 2^{p-1} \mathbb{E}\Big[ \left\|{S_1}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \Big] +  2^{p-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[ \left\|{S_2}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \Big],
     \label{CB:S1S2}
\end{align}
where, in the second line, we added 
\begin{align*}
   0 = \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} x \frac{\omega_\alpha(x)}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\overline{\nu}^N})}} d\nu(x) - \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} x \frac{\omega_\alpha(x)}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\overline{\nu}^N})}} d\nu(x)
\end{align*}
and used $(a+b)^p \leq 2^{p-1}(a^p+b^p)$.\newline
Now, we estimate each term separately, beginning with the first one: 
\begin{align}
   \mathbb{E}\Big[ \left\|{S_1}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \Big] &= \mathbb{E} \Bigg[  \left\|{  \frac{1}{N}\sum^N_{i=1} \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i\frac{\omega_\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i)}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\overline{\nu}^N})}}  -  \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} x \frac{\omega_\alpha(x)}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\overline{\nu}^N})}} d\nu(x)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \Bigg] \notag \\
   &=  \mathbb{E} \Bigg[  \bigg( \frac{1}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\overline{\nu}^N})}} \bigg)^p \left\|{\frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i=1} \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i\omega_\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i)  -  \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} x \omega_\alpha(x) d\nu(x)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \Bigg]. \notag
\end{align}
Since $p \geq 2$, Jensen's inequality yields on $\Omega_M$
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E}_{\overline{\nu}^N}\big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^2 \big] = \frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^N \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i}\right\|^2 \leq \bigg( \frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^N \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i}\right\|^p \bigg)^{\frac{2}{p}} \leq M^{\frac{2}{p}},
\end{align*}
so Lemma \ref{CarrL1} is applicable to $\overline{\nu}^N$ with $M_2 = M^{2/p}$.
We now expand the fraction that we pulled out by $\frac{\omega_\alpha(x^*)}{\omega_\alpha(x^*)}$ in the second line and use Lemma \ref{CarrL1} to further estimate:
\begin{align}
    & \mathbb{E} \Bigg[  \bigg( \frac{1}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\overline{\nu}^N})}} \bigg)^p \left\|{\frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i=1} \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i\omega_\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i)  -  \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} x \omega_\alpha(x) d\nu(x)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \Bigg] \notag \\ 
    = \, & \mathbb{E} \Bigg[  \bigg( \frac{\omega_\alpha(x^*)}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\overline{\nu}^N})}}\frac{1}{\omega_\alpha(x^*)} \bigg)^p \left\|{ \frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i=1} \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i\omega_\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i)  -  \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} x \omega_\alpha(x) d\nu(x)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \Bigg] \notag \\
  \text{(Lemma \ref{CarrL1})}  \quad  \leq \, & \mathbb{E} \Bigg[  \bigg( \frac{e^{\alpha C_2(1+M^{2/p})}}{ \omega_\alpha(x^*)} \bigg)^p \left\|{ \frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i=1} \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i\omega_\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i)  -  \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} x \omega_\alpha(x) d\nu(x)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \Bigg] \notag \\
      \leq \, & e^{ p \alpha (f(x^*)+C_2(1+M^{2/p}))} \mathbb{E}  \Bigg[ \left\|{ \frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i=1} \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i\omega_\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i)  -  \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} x \omega_\alpha(x) d\nu(x)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \Bigg],
      \label{CB:S1Bd}
\end{align}
where we used the second-moment bound on $\Omega_M$ from above.
We are left with bounding the non constant part. To this end, for all $i \leq N$ we introduce the random variables
\begin{align*}
    Y_i := \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i\omega_\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i)  -  \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} x \omega_\alpha(x) d\nu(x).
\end{align*}
Note that for all $i$, we have
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \big[ Y_i \big] = \underbrace{\mathbb{E} \Big[ \makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i\omega_\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i) \Big]}_{= \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} x \omega_\alpha(x) d\nu(x)}  -  \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} x \omega_\alpha(x) d\nu(x) = 0.
\end{align*}
This means that $\frac{1}{N}\sum^N_{i = 1} Y_i$ is the sample mean of independent, identically distributed, zero mean random variables with finite $p$th moments and we therefore can apply Corollary \ref{RosenthalCorr} to get the estimate
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \left\|{\frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i= 1} Y_i}\right\|^p \Bigg] \leq C_{p, d} \max \Bigg\{ \frac{\mathbb{E}\big[ \left\|{Y_1}\right\|^p \big]}{N^{p-1}},  \frac{\mathbb{E}\big[ \left\|{Y_1}\right\|^2 \big]^{\frac{p}{2}}}{N^{\frac{p}{2}}}  \Bigg \}, \label{CB:RosenthalEstimate} 
\end{align}
for a constant $C_{p, d} > 0$ that depends only on $p$ and $d$.
To further estimate, we use the definition of $Y_1$ to bound its $p$th moment
\begin{align*}
   \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{Y_1}\right\|^p \Big] &= \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^1 \omega_\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^1) - \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} x \omega_\alpha(x) d\nu(x)}\right\|^p \bigg]\\ &\leq 2^{p-1} \mathbb{E} \bigg[ \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^1}\right\|^p \omega_\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^1)^p + \left\|{\int_{\mathbb{R}^d} x \omega_\alpha(x) d\nu(x)}\right\|^p \bigg] \\
    &\leq 2^{p-1} \Bigg( \omega_\alpha(x^*)^p \mathbb{E}\Big[ \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^1}\right\|^p \Big] + \omega_\alpha(x^*)^p \mathbb{E}\Big[ \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^1}\right\|^p \Big] \Bigg).\\
    &=(2 \omega_\alpha(x^*))^p \mathbb{E}\Big[ \left\|{\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^1}\right\|^p \Big]\\
    &\leq (2 \omega_\alpha(x^*))^p M_p.
\end{align*}
Analogously, we get for the second moment of $Y_1$
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \Big[ \left\|{Y_1}\right\|^2 \Big] \leq 4 \omega_\alpha(x^*)^2 M_2
\end{align*}
By Jensen's inequality, we have that 
\begin{align*}
    (M_2)^{\frac{p}{2}} \leq M_p. 
\end{align*}
Combining the latter with our Rosenthal estimate \eqref{CB:RosenthalEstimate} using the monotonicity of expectation yields
\begin{align*}
     \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \left\|{\frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i= 1} Y_i}\right\|^p \Bigg] &\leq C_{p, d} \max \Bigg \{\frac{\big( 2\omega_\alpha(x^*) \big)^p M_p}{N^{p-1}}, \frac{\big( 2 \omega_\alpha(x^*) \big)^p (M_2)^{\frac{p}{2}}}{N^{\frac{p}{2}}} \Bigg \} \\ &\leq C_{p, d} M_p\big( 2 \omega_\alpha(x^*) \big)^p \max \Bigg \{ \frac{1}{N^{p-1}} , \frac{1}{N^{\frac{p}{2}}}\Bigg \}.
\end{align*}
We insert the latter into \eqref{CB:S1Bd} to get our final estimate for the first part:
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \big[ \left\|{S_1}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \big] \leq e^{ p \alpha (f(x^*)+C_2(1+M^{2/p}))} C_{p, d} M_p\big( 2\omega_\alpha(x^*) \big)^p \max \Bigg \{ \frac{1}{N^{p-1}} , \frac{1}{N^{\frac{p}{2}}}\Bigg \}. 
    \label{CB:S1FinalBound}
\end{align}
We now proceed to treat the second part of (\ref{CB:S1S2}). We start by expanding fractions to write the difference in terms of the consensus point of $\nu$ and then pulling it out.
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E}\big[ \left\|{S_2}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \big] &= \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \left\|{\int_{\mathbb{R}^d} x \frac{\omega_\alpha(x)}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\overline{\nu}^N})}} d\nu(x) - \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} x \frac{\omega_\alpha(x)}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\nu})}} d\nu(x)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \Bigg] \notag \\
  \Bigg( \text{expand by } \frac{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\nu})}}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\nu})}} \Bigg) \quad  &= \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \left\|{\frac{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\nu})}}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\overline{\nu}^N})}} X^\alpha(\nu) - X^\alpha(\nu)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \Bigg] \notag \\
  \big( \text{pull out } {X^\alpha}(\nu) \big) \quad   &= \mathbb{E} \Bigg[  \frac{\left\|{{X^\alpha}(\nu)}\right\|^p}{(\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\overline{\nu}^N})})^p} \Big|\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\nu})} - \left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\overline{\nu}^N})} \Big|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \Bigg].
    \label{CB:S2FirstBound}
\end{align}
We do a small auxiliary computation. With slight abuse of the notation and recalling that $\eta_{\nu} := \frac{\omega_\alpha \nu}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\nu})}}$ defines a probability measure on $\mathbb{R}^d$, we can write $\left\|{{X^\alpha}(\nu)}\right\|^p$ as
\begin{align*}
    \left\|{{X^\alpha}(\nu)}\right\|^p = \left\|{\int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \frac{x \omega_\alpha(x)}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\nu})}} d\nu}\right\|^p = \left\|{\int_{\mathbb{R}^d} x \,  d\eta_{\nu}(x)}\right\|^p = \left\|{\mathbb{E}_{\eta_{\nu}} \big[ x \big]}\right\|^p. 
\end{align*}
We can estimate this further by first using Jensen's inequality and after that Lemma \ref{GerberPropA4}:
\begin{align}
    \left\|{\mathbb{E}_{\eta_{\nu}} \big[ x \big]}\right\|^p \leq \mathbb{E}_{\eta_{\nu}} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big] \leq b_p \mathbb{E}_{\nu} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big]. 
    \label{CB:etaBound}
\end{align}
We insert (\ref{CB:etaBound}) into \eqref{CB:S2FirstBound}, so we can further compute:
\begin{align}
    & \quad \, \, \mathbb{E} \Bigg[  \frac{\left\|{{X^\alpha}(\nu)}\right\|^p}{(\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\overline{\nu}^N})})^p} \Big|\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\nu})} - \left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\overline{\nu}^N})} \Big|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \Bigg] \notag \\
 (\ref{CB:etaBound}) \quad   &\leq \mathbb{E} \Bigg[  \frac{b_p \mathbb{E}_{\nu} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big]}{(\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\overline{\nu}^N})})^p} \Big|\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\nu})} - \left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\overline{\nu}^N})} \Big|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \Bigg] \notag \\
  (\mathbb{E}_{\nu} \big[ \left\|{x}\right\|^p \big] \leq M_p) \quad &\leq  \mathbb{E} \Bigg[  \frac{b_p M_p}{(\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\overline{\nu}^N})})^p} \Big|\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\nu})} - \left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\overline{\nu}^N})} \Big|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \Bigg]
    \notag \\
\Bigg( \text{multiply } \frac{\omega_\alpha(x^*)}{\omega_\alpha(x^*)} \Bigg) \, \,   &= \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ b_p M_p \bigg( \frac{\omega_\alpha(x^*)}{\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\overline{\nu}^N})}} \frac{1}{\omega_\alpha(x^*)} \bigg)^p \Big|\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\nu})} - \left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\overline{\nu}^N})} \Big|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \Bigg] \notag \\
 (\text{Lemma \ref{CarrL1}}) \quad  &\leq \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ b_p M_p \bigg(  \frac{e^{\alpha C_2 (1+ M^{2/p})}}{\omega_\alpha(x^*)} \bigg)^p \Big|\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\nu})} -\left\|{\omega_\alpha}\right\|_{L^1({\overline{\nu}^N})} \Big|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \Bigg] \notag \\
 &\leq b_p M_p e^{ p \alpha (f(x^*)+C_2(1+M^{2/p}))} \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \Big| \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \omega_\alpha(x) d\nu(x) - \frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i=1} \omega_\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i) \Big|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \Bigg] \notag \\
    &\leq b_p M_p e^{ p \alpha (f(x^*)+C_2(1+M^{2/p}))} \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \Big| \frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i=1} \omega_\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i) - \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \omega_\alpha(x) d\nu(x)  \Big|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \Bigg].
    \label{CB:S2ConsBound}
\end{align}
We want to treat the expectation in \eqref{CB:S2ConsBound} in a similar way as the expectation in \eqref{CB:S1Bd} by applying Corollary \ref{RosenthalCorr}. Therefore, we define for every $i \leq N$
\begin{align*}
Z_i := \omega_\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i) - \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \omega_\alpha(x) d\nu(x).
\end{align*}
Note that for all $i$, we have
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \big[ Z_i \big] = \underbrace{\mathbb{E} \bigg[ \omega_\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^i) \bigg]}_{= \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \omega_\alpha(x) d\nu(x)}  -  \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \omega_\alpha(x) d\nu(x) = 0.
\end{align*}
This means that $\frac{1}{N}\sum^N_{i = 1} Z_i$ is the sample mean of independent, identically distributed random variables with finite $p$th moments and therefore we can apply Corollary \ref{RosenthalCorr} to get the estimate
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \left\|{\frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i= 1} Z_i}\right\|^p \Bigg] \leq C_{p, d} \max \Bigg \{\frac{\mathbb{E} \big[ \left\|{Z_1}\right\|^p \big]}{N^{p-1}} , \frac{\mathbb{E} \big[ \left\|{Z_1}\right\|^2 \big]^{\frac{p}{2}}}{N^{\frac{p}{2}}}\Bigg \}, \label{CB:RosenthalEstimate2} 
\end{align}
for a constant $C_{p, d} > 0$ that depends only on $p$ and $d$.
To further estimate, we use the definition of $Z_1$ to compute on a fixed path $\omega \in \Omega$
\begin{align*}
    \left\|{Z_1}\right\| = \left\|{ \omega_\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^1) - \int_{\mathbb{R}^d}  \omega_\alpha(x) d\nu(x)}\right\| &\leq  |\omega_\alpha(\makebox[0pt]{$\phantom{X}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phantom{X}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 0.2mu$}X^1)| + \bigg |\int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \omega_\alpha(x) d\nu(x) \bigg | \\
    &\leq  \omega_\alpha(x^*) + \omega_\alpha(x^*) = 2\omega_\alpha(x^*),
\end{align*}
where we used that $\omega_\alpha(x) = e^{-\alpha f(x)}$ attains its maximum at $x^*$.
Combining the latter with our Rosenthal estimate \eqref{CB:RosenthalEstimate2} yields
\begin{align*}
     \mathbb{E} \Bigg[ \left\|{\frac{1}{N} \sum^N_{i= 1} Z_i}\right\|^p \Bigg] &\leq C_{p, d} \max \Bigg \{\frac{\big( 2\omega_\alpha(x^*) \big)^p}{N^{p-1}} ,\frac{\big( 2 \omega_\alpha(x^*) \big)^p}{N^{\frac{p}{2}}} \Bigg \} \\ &= C_{p, d} \big( 2 \omega_\alpha(x^*) \big)^p \max \Bigg \{ \frac{1}{N^{p-1}} , \frac{1}{N^{\frac{p}{2}}}\Bigg \}.
\end{align*}
Combining this with \eqref{CB:S2ConsBound} gives us our final estimate for the second part:
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \big[ \left\|{S_2}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M} \big] \leq b_p M_p e^{ p \alpha (f(x^*)+C_2(1+M^{2/p}))} C_{p, d} \big( 2 \omega_\alpha(x^*) \big)^p \max \Bigg \{ \frac{1}{N^{p-1}} , \frac{1}{N^{\frac{p}{2}}}\Bigg \}. 
    \label{CB:S2FinalBound}
\end{align}
Finally, we can use (\ref{CB:S1FinalBound}) and (\ref{CB:S2FinalBound}) to get
\begin{align*}
     &\mathbb{E} \Bigg[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\bar{\nu}^N) - X^\alpha (\nu)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \Bigg]\\
     \leq \, & 2^{p-1}\Big( e^{ p \alpha (f(x^*)+C_2(1+M^{2/p}))} C_{p, d} M_p \big( 2 \omega_\alpha(x^*) \big)^p \\
     & \quad + b_p M_p e^{ p \alpha (f(x^*)+C_2(1+M^{2/p}))} C_{p, d} \big( 2 \omega_\alpha(x^*) \big)^p\Big) \max \Bigg \{ \frac{1}{N^{p-1}} , \frac{1}{N^{\frac{p}{2}}}\Bigg \}\\
     =\, & 2^{p-1} e^{ p \alpha (f(x^*)+C_2(1+M^{2/p}))}C_{p, d}(2\omega_\alpha(x^*))^p  \big( M_p + b_pM_p  \big) \max \Bigg \{ \frac{1}{N^{p-1}} , \frac{1}{N^{\frac{p}{2}}}\Bigg \}\\
     =: \, &C_{\mathrm{EM}}\max \Bigg \{ \frac{1}{N^{p-1}} , \frac{1}{N^{\frac{p}{2}}}\Bigg \},
\end{align*}
with the constant $C_{\mathrm{EM}} = C_{\mathrm{EM}}(\alpha, C_2, M, M_p, b_p, C_{p, d}, p)$. As we choose $p \geq 2$, we have 
\begin{align*}
   \max \Bigg \{ \frac{1}{N^{p-1}} , \frac{1}{N^{\frac{p}{2}}}\Bigg \} = \frac{1}{N^{\frac{p}{2}}}, 
\end{align*}
and therefore
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \Bigg[  \left\|{X^\alpha(\bar{\nu}^N) - X^\alpha (\nu)}\right\|^p \mathbbm{1}_{\Omega_M}  \Bigg] \leq C_{\mathrm{EM}} N^{-\frac{p}{2}}.
\end{align*}
This concludes the proof.
